//! Owner-only persistence, independent of the launching shell's umask.
//! Unix is the supported storage host; other targets fail closed until an
//! owner-only ACL implementation exists.
use std::fs::{self, File, OpenOptions};
use std::io;
#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::unix::fs::{DirBuilderExt, OpenOptionsExt, PermissionsExt};
use std::path::Path;

pub fn directory(path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    #[cfg(unix)]
    {
        fs::DirBuilder::new().recursive(true).mode(0o700).create(path)?;
        let meta = fs::symlink_metadata(path)?;
        if !meta.is_dir() || meta.file_type().is_symlink() {
            return Err(io::Error::other("private data directory must be a real directory"));
        }
        fs::set_permissions(path, fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o700))
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    {
        let _ = path;
        Err(io::Error::other("owner-only storage ACLs are not implemented on this host"))
    }
}

/// Create atomically with mode 0600. `new` forbids replacing an upload;
/// otherwise tighten an existing regular file before opening the store.
pub fn file(path: &Path, new: bool) -> io::Result<File> {
    #[cfg(unix)]
    {
        if let Ok(meta) = fs::symlink_metadata(path) {
            if !meta.is_file() || meta.file_type().is_symlink() {
                return Err(io::Error::other("private data file must be a regular file"));
            }
        }
        let mut options = OpenOptions::new();
        options.read(true).write(true).mode(0o600);
        if new {
            options.create_new(true);
        } else {
            options.create(true);
        }
        let opened = options.open(path)?;
        opened.set_permissions(fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600))?;
        Ok(opened)
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    {
        let _ = (path, new);
        Err(io::Error::other("owner-only storage ACLs are not implemented on this host"))
    }
}

pub fn existing_file(path: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    match fs::symlink_metadata(path) {
        Ok(meta) => {
            if !meta.is_file() || meta.file_type().is_symlink() {
                return Err(io::Error::other("private data file must be a regular file"));
            }
            #[cfg(unix)]
            { fs::set_permissions(path, fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600)) }
            #[cfg(not(unix))]
            { Err(io::Error::other("owner-only storage ACLs are not implemented on this host")) }
        }
        Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(()),
        Err(e) => Err(e),
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — ownership flocks belong beside SQLite, never on its database inode.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn owner_path(database: &Path) -> std::path::PathBuf {
    let mut name = database.as_os_str().to_owned();
    name.push("-owner");
    name.into()
}

/// Stat only: opening and closing a hard-linked database loses SQLite locks.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn database_metadata(database: &Path) -> io::Result<Option<fs::Metadata>> {
    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
    match fs::symlink_metadata(database) {
        Ok(meta) if !meta.is_file() => Err(io::Error::other("database must be a regular file")),
        Ok(meta) if meta.nlink() != 1 => Err(io::Error::other("database has hard links; copy the store, do not hard-link it")),
        Ok(meta) => Ok(Some(meta)),
        Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(None),
        Err(e) => Err(e),
    }
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn owner_file(database: &Path) -> io::Result<File> {
    auxiliary_file(database, &owner_path(database), true, 0)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — preflight aliases; an ambiguous descriptor must leak rather than close SQLite's POSIX locks.
/// Ownership files are empty; address records fit in 128 bytes. The size
/// bound also rejects a database renamed here after its original name vanished.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub fn auxiliary_file(database: &Path, path: &Path, create: bool, max_len: u64) -> io::Result<File> {
    let before = match fs::symlink_metadata(database) {
        Ok(meta) => Some(meta),
        Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => None,
        Err(e) => return Err(e),
    };
    match fs::symlink_metadata(path) {
        Ok(meta) => auxiliary_metadata(&meta, before.as_ref(), max_len)?,
        Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {},
        Err(e) => return Err(e),
    }
    let file = OpenOptions::new().read(true).write(create).create(create).truncate(false)
        .mode(0o600).custom_flags(libc::O_NOFOLLOW | libc::O_NONBLOCK).open(path)?;
    let file = checked_auxiliary(file, database, before.as_ref(), max_len)?;
    if create { file.set_permissions(fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600))?; }
    Ok(file)
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn auxiliary_metadata(meta: &fs::Metadata, database: Option<&fs::Metadata>, max_len: u64) -> io::Result<()> {
    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
    if !meta.is_file() || meta.nlink() != 1 || meta.len() > max_len
        || database.is_some_and(|db| (meta.dev(), meta.ino()) == (db.dev(), db.ino())) {
        Err(io::Error::other("ownership/address file must be a separate regular single-linked file of the expected size; stop the owners and restore the store and its sidecars"))
    } else { Ok(()) }
//! Ownership locks the database's sidecar; stat pins its inode. `serve.lock` is only an address hint.
use std::{fs::{self, File}, io::{Read, Seek, SeekFrom, Write}, path::{Path, PathBuf}, time::Duration};
use snapback4_core::{Engine, RuntimeError, Store};
#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;

pub(crate) struct Owner {
    _sidecar: std::sync::Arc<File>,
    identity: (u64, u64),
    path: PathBuf,
    record: Option<File>,
    reclaimed: Option<String>,
    pub(crate) token_authority: Option<crate::serve::token::Authority>,
}

/// The address `doctor` dials for the server that owns this store: the
/// published loopback address itself, or, for a wildcard bind, that family's
/// loopback on the same port. A published non-loopback address is not followed.
pub(crate) fn inspector_address(store: &Path) -> Option<std::net::SocketAddr> {
    let path = store.canonicalize().ok()?;
    dialable(&address(&path)?)
}

fn dialable(published: &str) -> Option<std::net::SocketAddr> {
    use std::net::{IpAddr, Ipv4Addr, Ipv6Addr, SocketAddr};
    let address = published.strip_prefix("http://")?.parse::<SocketAddr>().ok()?;
    let ip = match address.ip() {
        ip if ip.is_loopback() => ip,
        IpAddr::V4(ip) if ip.is_unspecified() => IpAddr::V4(Ipv4Addr::LOCALHOST),
        IpAddr::V6(ip) if ip.is_unspecified() => IpAddr::V6(Ipv6Addr::LOCALHOST),
        _ => return None,
    };
    (address.port() != 0).then(|| SocketAddr::new(ip, address.port()))
}

fn resolve(store: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    match store.canonicalize() {
        Ok(path) => Ok(path),
        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
            let parent = store.parent().filter(|p| !p.as_os_str().is_empty()).unwrap_or(Path::new("."));
            snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(parent).map_err(|e| failure(store, e))?;
            Ok(parent.canonicalize().map_err(|e| failure(store, e))?.join(store.file_name().ok_or("store path has no filename")?))
        }
        Err(e) => Err(failure(store, e)),
    }
}

fn failure(path: &Path, reason: impl std::fmt::Display) -> String {
    format!("E_STORE at {}: {reason}; restore a regular store/address file and retry", path.display())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn same_file(a: &fs::Metadata, b: &fs::Metadata) -> bool { a.dev() == b.dev() && a.ino() == b.ino() }

#[cfg(unix)]
fn regular(path: &Path, create: bool, database: &Path) -> Result<File, String> {
    snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(database, path, create, 128)
        .map_err(|e| failure(path, e))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — sidecar flocks fence backend writers and share device ownership without locking SQLite.
pub(crate) fn command(store: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> { acquire(store, false, Duration::ZERO) }
pub(crate) fn device(store: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> { acquire(store, true, Duration::ZERO) }
// @ref LLP 3000.009#5.3 — a restarting host waits for watchdogs to finish group cleanup.
pub(crate) fn host(store: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> { acquire(store, false, Duration::from_secs(5)) }

#[cfg(unix)]
fn acquire(store: &Path, shared: bool, wait: Duration) -> Result<Owner, String> {
    let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + wait;
    for _ in 0..16 {
        let path = resolve(store)?;
        snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(path.parent().ok_or("store path has no parent")?)
            .map_err(|e| failure(&path, e))?;
        // Stat before opening either auxiliary file; a refused probe creates
        // no database. Only the sidecar lock grants permission to create one.
        let before = snapback4_core::private_fs::database_metadata(&path).map_err(|e| failure(&path, e))?;
        let sidecar = snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_file(&path)
            .map_err(|e| failure(&snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_path(&path), e))?;
        let mut cleanup = None;
        loop {
            let locked = if shared { sidecar.try_lock_shared() } else { sidecar.try_lock() };
            match locked {
                Ok(()) => break,
                Err(fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) if std::time::Instant::now() < deadline && *cleanup.get_or_insert_with(|| stopped_host(&path)) => std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(25)),
                Err(fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) => return Err(busy(&path)),
                Err(fs::TryLockError::Error(error)) => return Err(failure(&path, error)),
            }
        }
        if !snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_is_named(&path, &sidecar) { continue; }
        snapback4_core::private_fs::database_file(&path).map_err(|e| failure(&path, e))?;
        let database = snapback4_core::private_fs::database_metadata(&path).map_err(|e| failure(&path, e))?
            .ok_or_else(|| failure(&path, "database disappeared during ownership acquisition"))?;
        if before.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| !same_file(old, &database)) { continue; }
        let mut reclaimed = None;
        let record = if shared { None } else {
            let record_path = path.with_file_name("serve.lock");
            if let Some(pid)=owner_pid(&path) {
                if unsafe {libc::kill(pid as libc::pid_t,0)} == -1 && std::io::Error::last_os_error().raw_os_error()==Some(libc::ESRCH) {
                    reclaimed = Some(format!("reclaimed stale serve.lock: dead PID {pid}; directory {}", path.parent().unwrap().display()));
                }
            }
            let file = regular(&record_path, true, &path)?;
            file.set_len(0).map_err(|e| failure(&record_path, e))?;
            Some(file)
        };
        return Ok(Owner { _sidecar: std::sync::Arc::new(sidecar), identity: (database.dev(), database.ino()), path, record, reclaimed, token_authority: None });
    }
    Err(failure(store, "database path kept changing during ownership acquisition"))
}

#[cfg(not(unix))]
fn acquire(store: &Path, _: bool, _: Duration) -> Result<Owner, String> { Err(failure(store, "database ownership requires Unix")) }

pub(crate) fn stopped_host(path: &Path) -> bool {
    // A CLI owner or a host not yet announced still refuses immediately. A
    // reachable listener does too; only a prior host's cleanup gets a wait.
    // This is a refusal/wait hint, never permission to open the store: the
    // sidecar must still be acquired. Never dial a non-loopback address hint.
    address(path).is_some_and(|published| dialable(&published).is_none_or(|address|
        std::net::TcpStream::connect_timeout(&address, Duration::from_millis(50)).is_err()))
}

pub(crate) fn busy(path: &Path) -> String {
    let owner = address(path).map(|address| match owner_pid(path) { Some(pid) => format!("{address} (pid {pid})"), None => address })
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "another CLI command or a server starting up (address pending)".into());
    format!("E_STORE_BUSY at {}: this store is live; owned by {owner}; directory {}; no work was admitted. Stop its `snapback4 dev` or `snapback4 serve` process (or wait for the owning CLI command), rerun this command once, then restart the server. Exclusion holds while <database>-owner is left in place; deleting or replacing it while a process runs ends exclusion and does not stop that process", path.display(),path.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new(".")).display())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn address(path: &Path) -> Option<String> {
    // A missing/replaced address record cannot grant ownership. Never follow
    // a symlink (including one to the database), nor block on a FIFO.
    let record = path.with_file_name("serve.lock");
    let file = regular(&record, false, path).ok()?;
    let mut text = String::new();
    file.take(1024).read_to_string(&mut text).ok()?;
    let mut lines = text.lines();
    let port = lines.next().and_then(|s| s.parse::<u16>().ok());
    lines.next().map(str::to_owned).or_else(|| port.map(|p| format!("http://127.0.0.1:{p}")))
}

/// The owning host's PID from its address record (`pid N` on the third line).
#[cfg(unix)]
fn owner_pid(path: &Path) -> Option<u32> {
    let record = path.with_file_name("serve.lock");
    let file = regular(&record, false, path).ok()?;
    let mut text = String::new();
    file.take(1024).read_to_string(&mut text).ok()?;
    text.lines().nth(2)?.strip_prefix("pid ")?.parse().ok()
}
        if !self.personas { return false; }
        let bound = lock(&self.bound);
        self.unsafe_dev_doors || !(bound.schema.auth.password && !bound.schema.auth.guests)
    }
    // Revalidate under the writer: neither a slow body nor an adoption queue
    // may carry fixture authority into a newly password-only schema.
    pub(crate) fn dev_authority(&self, engine: &Engine) -> Result<(), Refusal> {
        if self.personas && (self.unsafe_dev_doors || !(engine.schema().auth.password && !engine.schema().auth.guests)) { Ok(()) }
        else { Err(dev_doors::closed()) }
    }
    pub fn now(&self) -> i64 { lock(&self.clock).now().max(self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire)) }
    pub(crate) fn refresh_time_audience_clock(&self, engine: &Engine, now: i64) {
        if engine.schema().tables.values().any(|table| table.not_before.is_some()) {
            let durable = engine.store().meta("time_audience_clock").ok().flatten()
                .and_then(|value| value.parse::<i64>().ok()).unwrap_or(i64::MIN);
            self.time_audience_clock.fetch_max(now.max(durable), Ordering::AcqRel);
        } else {
            self.time_audience_clock.store(i64::MIN, Ordering::Release);
        }
    }
    fn advance_time_audiences(&self) -> Result<i64, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        let requested = lock(&self.clock).now();
        // Non-timed reader traffic must retain its independent reader lane,
        // including while adoption or a mutation deliberately holds the writer.
        if self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire) == i64::MIN { return Ok(requested); }
        let mut engine = lock(&self.engine);
        let before = engine.seq()?;
        let now = engine.advance_time_audiences_at(requested)?;
        self.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, now);
        if engine.seq()? != before { self.durable_committed(&engine); }
        Ok(now)
    }
    fn next_time_audience(&self) -> Result<Option<i64>, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        if self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire) == i64::MIN { return Ok(None); }
        lock(&self.engine).next_time_audience_at()
    }
    fn time_audience_delay(&self, at: i64) -> Duration {
        let raw = lock(&self.clock).now();
        let floor = self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire);
        Duration::from_millis(if at <= floor { 0 } else { at.saturating_sub(raw).max(0) }.try_into().unwrap_or(u64::MAX))
    }
    pub(crate) fn worker_owner(&self) -> Option<Arc<std::fs::File>> { self.owner.as_ref().map(|owner| owner.worker_lock()) }
}

impl Table {
    pub fn index(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&Index> {
        self.indexes.get(name)
    }

    pub fn cap(&self, by: &[String]) -> Option<u32> {
        self.constraints.iter().find_map(|constraint| match constraint {
            Constraint::Cap { by: group, max } if group == by && !self.cap_exempts_null(group) => Some(*max),
            _ => None,
        })
    }

    /// A cap grouping by a `set null` reference does not count its null rows
    /// (LLP 3000.039 §2.2), so it proves no population bound.
    pub fn cap_exempts_null(&self, by: &[String]) -> bool {
        by.iter().any(|column| self.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&references::OnDelete::SetNull))
    }

    pub fn immutable_fields(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
        self.constraints.iter().flat_map(|constraint| match constraint {
            Constraint::Immutable { fields } => fields.iter().map(String::as_str).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
            _ => Vec::new(),
        })
    }

    /// Whether `row` is past its `expire` column at `now`: absent to every
    /// reader, pending the sweep. A null time never expires.
    pub fn expired(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.deadline(row).is_some_and(|at| at <= now)
    }

    /// The index the expiry sweep walks: the first leading with the
    /// `expire` column, as the declaration requires.
    pub fn expiry_index(&self) -> Option<(&str, &Index)> {
        let column = self.expiry_column()?;
        self.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
                index.is_plain() && index.columns().first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
            }).map(|(name, index)| (name.as_str(), index))
    }

    /// The column carrying the principal a `sync to T[...]` audience reads.
    pub fn principal_columns(&self) -> Vec<&str> {
        self.columns.iter().filter(|(_, kind)| matches!(kind, ColumnType::Principal)).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect()
    }

        }
        // Time remains advanced if maintenance fails; callers must not
        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":skew, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "denied":refusal_json(&error)})),

/// `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}`: queue one run of a job as the
/// system on the dev clock and drive chunks until it is done or parked. This is
/// what `snapback4 run <job>` forwards to while dev owns the store.
pub(super) fn run_job(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
    use snapback4_core::{JobState, error::Refusal, ir::ProgramKind, wire};
    let service_route=request.url().starts_with("/jobs/");
    let service=if service_route {
        match super::session_identity::principal_of(&request,shared) {
            Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind=="service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Some(principal),
            _ => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","triggering a deployed system job requires a full service token").guide("jobs"))})),
        }
    } else {
        if !shared.personas {return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","/dev/jobs is a development door; use run <job> --release <dir> with a service token").guide("jobs"))}));}
        None
    };
    let refuse = |request, status, refusal: Refusal| respond(request, status, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&refusal.guide("jobs"))}));
    let payload: serde_json::Value = match serde_json::from_str(if body.trim().is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(error) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("the request body is not JSON: {error}; send {{\"args\":{{..}}}}"))),
    };
    if service_route && !shared.personas {crate::release_requests::Scope::admit(&shared.release_requests,||*lock(&shared.generation));}
    let program = lock(&shared.bound).programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == name).map(|p| p.program.clone());
    let program = match program {
        Some(program) if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job => program,
        Some(other) => return refuse(request, 404, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("{name} is a {}, not a job; queries are POST /q/<op>, mutations POST /m/<op>", other.kind.word()))),
        None => return respond(request, 404, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&super::dev_doors::stale(shared, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("unknown job {name}")).guide("jobs")))})),
    };
    if let Some(raw)=payload.get("args") && let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&program.args,raw) {return refuse(request,400,why);}
    let mut args = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    if let Some(fields) = payload.get("args").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object) {
        for (field, value) in fields {
            let Some(kind) = program.args.get(field) else { return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{name} takes no argument {field}"))); };
            match wire::from_json(kind, value) {
                Ok(typed) => { args.insert(field.clone(), typed); }
                Err(why) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {field}: {why}"))),
            }
        }
    }
    let mut admitted=lock(&shared.engine);
    crate::release_requests::stamp(admitted.generation());
    if !service_route && let Err(why) = shared.dev_authority(&admitted) { drop(admitted); return refuse(request,403,why); }
    if let Some(service)=&service {
        if let Err(why)=super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&admitted,service) {drop(admitted);return refuse(request,403,why);}
    }
    let queued=admitted.enqueue_job(name,args,shared.now());
    drop(admitted);
    crate::release_requests::executed();
    let id = match queued {
        Ok(id) => id,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
    };
    // FIFO: earlier queued runs finish first, as with `run` on a stopped store;
    // the background tick may run some chunks too, through the same engine.
    let run = loop {
        let current = {let engine=lock(&shared.engine);crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());engine.job(&id)};
        match current {
            Ok(Some(run)) if matches!(run.state, JobState::Done | JobState::Parked) || crate::lifecycle::stopping() => break run,
            Ok(Some(_)) => {}
            Ok(None) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&RuntimeError::Store(format!("job {id} vanished")).refusal())})),
            Err(error) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
        }
        let (chunk, logs) = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| (engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget), engine.take_native_logs()));
        for line in logs { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
        match chunk {
            Ok(Some(chunk)) if chunk.state == JobState::Parked && shared.personas => shared.refusals.job(&chunk),
            Ok(_) => {}
            Err(error) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
        }
    };
    snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} {} on request: {} chunk{}, {} row{} written", run.name, if run.state == JobState::Done { "done" } else { "parked" },
        run.chunks, if run.chunks == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, run.rows_written, if run.rows_written == 1 { "" } else { "s" });
    respond(request, 200, json!({ "job": run.name, "id": run.id, "state": run.state, "chunks": run.chunks, "chunkSize": run.chunk_size, "retries": run.retries, "heapPeak": run.heap_peak, "rowsWritten": run.rows_written, "error": run.error }));
}

pub(super) fn clock_status(request: Request, shared: &Shared) {
    if let Err(why) = clock_service(&request, shared) { return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})); }
    let result = (|| {
        let (epoch, reader) = shared.reader()?;
        let note = crate::dev_clock_store::ahead(&reader, shared.now());
        lock(&shared.readers).put(&epoch, reader.into_inner());
        note
    })();
    match result {
        Ok(note) => { let now = shared.now(); respond(request, 200, json!({"storeAhead":note,"now":now,"skewMs":now - snapback4_core::now_ms()})) },
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
    }
}

    /// How many entries sit under `prefix`, counting at most `limit`.
    pub fn count_prefix(&self, table: &str, index_name: &str, prefix: &[Value], limit: u64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        let ti = self.num(&format!("{table}.{index_name}"))?;
        let base = key::encode(prefix);
        let hi = key::upper_bound(&base);
        let count: i64 = self.tx.prepare_cached("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (SELECT 1 FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 AND k >= ?2 AND k < ?3 LIMIT ?4)")?.query_row(params![ti, base, hi, limit as i64], |row| row.get(0))?;
        Ok(count as u64)
    }

    /// Write a row image: a fresh insert when `old` is none, else a replace
    /// that touches only the index entries whose keys changed (a `words`
    /// index has one per token). Returns the number of entries written.
    pub fn put(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value, old: Option<&Value>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.put_indexed(table_name, table, row, old, "INSERT INTO i(ti, k, id) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)")
    }

    /// A replacement loader has already cleared the rows and created its
    /// temporary index heap. It must install those indexes before any read.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.4 — native replacement sorts complete indexes inside the publication transaction.
    pub fn put_loading_indexes(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.put_indexed(table_name, table, row, None, "INSERT INTO temp._device_index_load(ti, k, id) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)")
    }

    fn put_indexed(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value, old: Option<&Value>, insert_index: &str) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.clear_activation_scan();
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { return Err(RuntimeError::Store("durable transaction cannot write an ephemeral table".into())); }
        let t = self.num(table_name)?;
        let id = row.field("id")?.as_str()?;
        narrowed::check_write(self,table_name,table,row,old)?;
        backfills::observe_write(self,t,id,table,row,old)?;
        let keys = crate::rows::index_write(table_name, table, row, old, id)?;
        self.changes.borrow_mut().record(table_name, table, old, Some(row));
        let data = canonical_json(row);
        let sql = if old.is_some() { "UPDATE r SET data = ?3 WHERE t = ?1 AND id = ?2" } else { "INSERT INTO r(t, id, data) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)" };
        self.tx.prepare_cached(sql)?.execute(params![t, id, data])?;
        let mut insert = self.tx.prepare_cached(insert_index)?;
        let mut entries = 0;
        for crate::rows::IndexWrite { name, before, after } in keys {
            if before == after { continue; }
            let ti = self.num(&format!("{table_name}.{name}"))?;
            for key in before.iter().filter(|key| !after.contains(key)) {
                self.conn().prepare_cached("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 AND k = ?2")?.execute(params![ti, key])?;
                entries += 1;
            }
            for key in after.iter().filter(|key| !before.contains(key)) {
                insert.execute(params![ti, key, id])?;
                entries += 1;
            }
        }
        entries += self.update_cap_counts(table_name, table, old, Some(row))?;
        Ok(entries)
    }

    pub fn delete(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, old: &Value) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.clear_activation_scan();
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { return Err(RuntimeError::Store("durable transaction cannot write an ephemeral table".into())); }
        let t = self.num(table_name)?;
        self.changes.borrow_mut().record(table_name, table, Some(old), None);

        dormant::delete(self,t,&id,table_name)?;
        self.conn().prepare_cached("DELETE FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id = ?2")?.execute(params![t, id])?;
        let mut entries = 0;
        for (index_name, index) in &table.indexes {
            let ti = self.num(&format!("{table_name}.{index_name}"))?;
            for key in stored_index_keys(table, index, old, &id) {
                self.conn().prepare_cached("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 AND k = ?2")?.execute(params![ti, key])?;
                entries += 1;
            }
        }
        entries += self.update_cap_counts(table_name, table, Some(old), None)?;
        Ok(entries)

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — staging grants clock control, never fixture or persona authority.
pub(super) fn staging(shared: &Shared) -> bool {
    lock(&shared.engine).store().meta("deployment_environment").ok().flatten().as_deref() == Some("staging")
}
fn clock_service(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<Option<String>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if shared.personas { return Ok(None); }
    if !staging(shared) { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
    match super::session_identity::principal_of(request,shared) {
        Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind == "service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Ok(Some(principal)),
        _ => Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "staging clock control requires a full service token").guide("clock")),
    }
}

            || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| {
                t.fields.contains_key(field) || matches!(field, DEADLINE | PARENT_DEADLINE)
            })
    }
    /// One classification for every expiry gate, including inherited deadlines.
    pub fn is_expiring(&self) -> bool {
        self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.expire.is_some() || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.expiring)
    }
    pub fn expiry_column(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        if self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.through.is_some() && self.is_expiring() {
            Some(DEADLINE)
        } else {
            self.expire.as_deref()
        }
    }
    pub fn deadline(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        match self
            .expiry_column()
            .and_then(|field| row_image(row, self).field(field).ok().cloned())
        {
            Some(Value::Int(at)) => Some(at),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    /// Never trust a restored effective instant; recompute from immutable capture.
    pub fn recompute_deadline(&self, row: &mut Value) -> Result<(), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
        if self.through.is_none() {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let authored = self
            .expire
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|c| match row_image(row, self).field(c) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                _ => None,
            });
        let parent = match row.field(PARENT_DEADLINE)? {
            Value::Int(at) => Some(*at),
            Value::Null => None,
            _ => {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Input(
                    "captured parent deadline must be a time or null".into(),
                ));
            }
        };
                match super::program::validate_code(&index.site, code) {
                    Ok(()) => {
                        schema.unique_refusals.entry(info.decl.name.clone()).or_default().insert(index.name.clone(), core::UniqueRefusal {
                            code: code.clone(), message: message.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| format!("the program refused with {code}")),
                        });
                    }
                    Err(diagnostic) => diagnostics.push(diagnostic),
                }
            }
        }
        schema.tables.insert(info.decl.name.clone(), table);
    }
    // Identity is semantic, independent of source locations and index planning order.
    for name in schema.through_order() {
        let Some(t) = schema.tables[&name].through.clone() else { continue; };
        let parent=&schema.tables[&t.parent];
        let fields: Vec<_> = t.fields.values().map(|f| (f,parent.columns.get(f),parent.defaults.get(f))).collect();
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — expiry interpretation changes recapture every descendant, even when the rule never reads the deadline.
        let expiry = parent.expire.as_ref().map(|field| (field, parent.columns.get(field), parent.defaults.get(field)));
        let identity=snapback4_core::value::digest(&(&parent.rules.read,fields,expiry,&parent.through,&schema.tables[&name].columns[&t.reference],schema.tables[&name].defaults.get(&t.reference)));
        schema.tables.get_mut(&name).unwrap().through.as_mut().unwrap().identity=if t.live.is_some() { snapback4_core::value::digest(&(identity,t.live)) } else { identity };
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#6.7 — inherit the resolved audience, unless explicitly online only.
    for maintain in catalog.maintains.values() {
        if maintain.row_key.is_some() || maintain.online_only || !schema.tables.contains_key(&maintain.name) { continue; }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#5.4 — an aggregate over a union source is answered by the server: it inherits no audience.
        let sync = schema.tables.get(&maintain.source).and_then(|t| t.sync.clone()).filter(|sync| !sync.is_union());
        if let Some(sync) = sync {
            let aliases = super::maintains::inherited_aliases(&catalog.tables[&maintain.source], maintain);
            let output = |column: &String| aliases.iter().find_map(|(out, capture)| (capture == column).then(|| out.clone()))
                .or_else(|| maintain.keys.iter().any(|key| matches!(key, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == column)).then(|| column.clone()));
            let audience = match &sync.audience {
                Audience::Public => Some(Audience::Public),
                Audience::Column(column) => output(column).map(Audience::Column),
                Audience::Target { table, prefix, principal } => prefix.iter().map(output).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>().map(|prefix| Audience::Target { table: table.clone(), prefix, principal: principal.clone() }),
            };
            if let Some(audience) = audience {
                let target = schema.tables.get_mut(&maintain.name).expect("bound maintain");
                target.sync = Some(Sync { audience, horizon: None, also: Vec::new() });
                target.residency.inherited_from = Some(maintain.source.clone());
            }
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
authoring census; the 94 private eligible sites stay unchanged.

Reports and all 150 schema hashes: `reports/20261018-sec/`. Main 20k and device
0–999 differentials have no disagreements; Lean's four known signature classes
retain the exact integration15 counts. Whole-protocol/device freshness,
general aggregate/time reductions, actual unauthenticated/revoked-device
campaigns and the repaired 015 custody workflow remain explicit gaps in
`../issues/20261018-sb4-security-proof-and-adversarial-qualification-gaps.md`.
This is a conservative verification foundation, not a zero-holes capability
claim. No version bump, push or publication.


## 2026-10-19 — MIG staging clock control

Classified staging stores admit serve --clock-offset and the existing clock
advance/status wire under a full service token. Production refuses offsets
before listener startup and all HTTP clock control even with operator authority.
The writer rechecks classification/session before advancing. startServe exposes
clockOffset, now and advance; it mints a current staging-clock operator session
for each control rather than caching a token across arbitrarily large jumps.
Fixture/reset/persona/job dev doors remain closed. The native-backed client
regression passed, including CLI advancement and restart dropping the offset.
The installed-package staging qualifier now exercises the same clock helpers.
LLP 3000.000.000.002 records the combined MIG language/adoption amendment;
independent review continues before final acceptance.


## 2026-10-19 — MIG declarative backfills and device bytes

Backfills are typed scalar patches evaluated before candidate narrowing, with
per-lifetime preimages and declaration membership retained through dormancy.
The existing activation transaction stages every result, checks historical
lookup uniqueness, then publishes data/indexes/derived state and generation.
Whole-table and narrowed restoration join the source snapshot. Hot adoption
and exact previews sample the serving clock, including staging offsets.

The web schema omits server-only migration metadata and evidence. Locked Linux
measurement is 2,385,285 B raw / 726,681 B gzip, +4,796 raw / +679 gzip versus
Integration 17. The small deterministic text library must use the same Rust
interpreter on server and device; the artifact allowance moves to measured
727,181 B (exactly 500 B headroom). No runtime meter ceiling changes. Family
and absent-feature audits and final qualification are recorded in the MIG
report; this measurement alone does not claim those checks have passed.

2026-10-19 MIG final wasm: metered text argument accounting measures 2,385,447 raw / 726,623 gzip bytes; budget tightened to 727,123 (measurement +500). Server-only backfill metadata remains absent from web wasm.

//! Preflight activation pages before owning their serialized row images.
use super::*;
use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}};

pub(super) fn walk(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    walk_metered(tx, table, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, row, _| visit(id, row))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — reserve stored bytes before fetching or decoding; page scratch stays live until consumed.
pub(super) fn walk_metered(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work, scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value) -> crate::value::InternalPrice>,
    visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let checked=|row:&Value|Ok(scratch.map_or_else(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero,|f|f(row)));
    walk_metered_checked(tx,table,work,scratch.map(|_|&checked as &dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>),visit)
}

/// Row-specific provenance can fail closed before any page callback runs.
pub(super) fn walk_metered_checked(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work,
    scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>>,
    mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    const PAGE: usize = 256;
    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    loop {
        // One job meter per page, shared with contribution evaluation. Retain
        // the continuation's allowance across the page; reset only after all
        // its row images and callback scratch have been consumed and dropped.
        let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
        meter.at("adopt stored rows");
        meter.heap(after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
        meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
        let page_mark = meter.mark();
        let mut limit = PAGE;
        let mut page = loop {
            limit = work.limit(&meter, limit);
            let sizes = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let (mut count, mut bytes) = (0, 0u64);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(sizes)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, limit as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                    let id_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize;
                    let data_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize;
                    // Rust-owned text, two native payload copies, allocation
                    // headers and exact Vec slots. Length preflight borrows no
                    // overflow payload (the same SQLite convention as scans).
                    let next = bytes.saturating_add(3 * (heap::allocation(id_bytes) + heap::allocation(data_bytes)) + 64 + heap::slots::<(String, String)>(1));
                    // This first size estimate bounds the fetch; admission below
                    // also checks the actual decoder and contribution allowance.
                    if count > 0 && next > meter.ceilings.heap_bytes / 2 { break; }
                    meter.heap(next - bytes)?;
                    bytes = next;
                    count += 1;
                }
            }
            if count == 0 { return Ok(()); }
            let query = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let mut page = Vec::<(String, String)>::with_capacity(count);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(query)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, count as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? { page.push((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)); }
            }
            if adoption::page_fits_checked(page.iter().map(|(_, data)| data.as_str()), &mut meter, work, scratch)? { break page; }
            limit = (page.len() / 2).max(1);
            drop(page);
            meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
        };
        for (id, data) in &page {
            let row_mark = meter.mark();
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            // Decode plus copy-on-write projection, optional materialization
            // and validation scratch. Contribution keys reserve more through
            // this same meter, on top of the still-resident complete page.
            adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
            let row = serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            visit(id, row, &mut meter)?;
            meter.release_heap(row_mark);
        }
        // Move the last ID into the next page's continuation, without an
        // unpriced clone. Drop every other fetched byte before releasing.
        after = page.pop().map(|row| row.0);
        drop(page);
        meter.release_heap(page_mark);
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn fixture(count: usize, payload: usize) -> Store {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        for n in 0..count {
            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(payload))),
            ]));
            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
        }
        tx.commit().unwrap();
        store
    }

    #[test]
    fn callback_keeps_the_whole_fetched_page_reserved() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let mut serialized_bytes = 0;
        for n in 0..20 {
            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(1024))),
            ]));
            serialized_bytes += (id.len() + data.len()) as u64;
            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
        }
        let mut calls = 0;
        let mut first_heap = None;
        walk_metered(&tx, 1, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |_, row, meter| {
            let mut decoder = Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort);
            adoption::charge(&mut decoder, adoption::decode_bytes(&canonical_json(&row)))?;
            let decode = decoder.used.heap_bytes;
            // Independent lower bound: Rust-owned images and both native
            // payload copies coexist with decode. This excludes headers/slots,
            // so it does not just repeat the walker's allocation formula.
            assert!(meter.used.heap_bytes >= 3 * serialized_bytes + decode,
                "Rust and native page copies must coexist with decoder scratch: {:?}", meter.used);
            let expected = first_heap.get_or_insert(meter.used.heap_bytes);
            assert_eq!(meter.used.heap_bytes, *expected, "earlier rows release only their own scratch");
            assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, 20 + calls + 1, "all admission decodes plus callback decodes are charged");
            calls += 1;
            Ok(())
        }).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(calls, 20);
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn heap_retries_keep_admission_examinations_at_the_callback_boundary() {
        let mut store = fixture(5, 1280 << 10);
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let work = adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 1, index_entries: 1 };
        let scratch = |row: &Value| adoption::contribution_bytes(row, &crate::schema::public_table(Default::default(), Default::default()));
        let mut observed = Vec::new();
        walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, Some(&scratch), |_, row, meter| {
            observed.push(meter.used.examined);
            assert_eq!((meter.used.steps, meter.used.index_entries), (0, 0), "rejected attempts perform no contribution work");
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            meter.step(1)?;
            adoption::charge(meter, scratch(&row))?;
            meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, 1)?;
            assert!(meter.heap_peak <= Ceilings::job().heap_bytes);
            Ok(())
        }).unwrap();
        // Four two-image attempts each decode one row before the heap check
        // asks for a singleton. Its admission and callback decode add two.
        // The last source is already a singleton and needs no rejected attempt.
        assert_eq!(observed, [3, 3, 3, 3, 2]);
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_cumulative_budget_fixes_the_page_before_any_callback() {
        for (work, sizes) in [
            (adoption::Work { examined: 64, steps: 63, index_entries: 63 }, vec![252, 4]),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 5040, index_entries: 1 }, vec![198, 58]),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 0, index_entries: 400 }, vec![40, 40, 40, 40, 40, 40, 16]),
        ] {
            let mut store = fixture(256, 0);
            let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
            let (mut page, mut offset, mut calls) = (0, 0u64, 0);
            walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, None, |_, _, meter| {
                assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, sizes[page] + offset * work.examined + 1,
                    "the entire admission pass and every earlier callback remain charged");
                assert_eq!(meter.used.steps, offset * work.steps);
                assert_eq!(meter.used.index_entries, offset * work.index_entries);
                meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, work.examined - 1)?;
                meter.step(work.steps)?;
                meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, work.index_entries)?;
                calls += 1;
                offset += 1;
                if offset == sizes[page] { page += 1; offset = 0; }

//! The SQLite store. Two generic tables carry every application table: `r`
//! holds row images by (table number, id) and `i` holds every index entry as
//! one order-preserving key (`key.rs`) so a prefix scan, a point lookup and
//! a cursor position are all one B-tree range. The rest is the engine's own
//! bookkeeping: the catalog of table and index numbers, commits, the
//! partition log, applied operation ids, principals, sessions, generations.

const STORE_VERSION: &str = "snapback4-store-1";

#[cfg(unix)]
fn file_identity(conn: &Connection) -> Result<Option<(u64, u64)>, RuntimeError> {
    use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
    conn.path().filter(|path| !path.is_empty()).map(|path| {
        std::fs::metadata(path).map(|m| (m.dev(), m.ino()))
            .map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(format!("database identity at {path}: {e}")))
    }).transpose()
}

pub struct Store {
    conn: Connection,
    /// Server stores own timed publication. Device stores receive only rows
    /// the server has already published and never consult this private ledger.
    server: bool,
    // Fields drop in declaration order: retain the sidecar ownership lock until
    // SQLite has closed. No raw descriptor to the database is held here.
    ownership: Option<std::sync::Arc<std::fs::File>>,
    #[cfg(unix)]
    file_identity: Option<(u64, u64)>,
    page_size: u64,
    catalog: BTreeMap<String, i64>,
    /// Where uploaded bytes live: `assets/` beside the database file, or a
    /// directory that dies with the store for a store in memory.
    assets_dir: PathBuf,
    /// A store in memory owns its assets directory: nothing creates it until
    /// the first upload, and dropping the store removes it. Before this, every
    /// `Store::memory()` created one and nothing removed it — one empty
    /// directory per in-memory engine, four million of them in a week of test
    /// runs on one machine, until listing the temp directory took minutes.
    ephemeral_assets: bool,
    identity: activation::Identity,
    #[cfg(test)]
    full_adoption: bool,
    pub adoption_report: adoption_report::AdoptionReport,
}

impl Drop for Store {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if self.ephemeral_assets {
            let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.assets_dir);
        }
    }
}

/// One transaction over the store, with the catalog in reach.
pub struct Tx<'a> {
    activation_scan: Option<activation_scan::Buffer>,
    pub changes: std::cell::RefCell<crate::revocation::Recorder>,
    sequence_table: std::cell::Cell<Option<bool>>,
    tx: rusqlite::Transaction<'a>,
    page_size: u64,
    server: bool,
    catalog: &'a BTreeMap<String, i64>,
}

fn scan_sql(dir: Dir) -> &'static str {
    match dir {
        Dir::Asc => {
            "SELECT r.data, i.k FROM i JOIN r ON r.t = ?1 AND r.id = i.id WHERE i.ti = ?2 AND i.k >= ?3 AND i.k < ?4 ORDER BY i.k ASC LIMIT ?5"
        }
        Dir::Desc => {
            "SELECT r.data, i.k FROM i JOIN r ON r.t = ?1 AND r.id = i.id WHERE i.ti = ?2 AND i.k >= ?3 AND i.k < ?4 ORDER BY i.k DESC LIMIT ?5"
        }
    }
}

impl Store {
    /// Inspect a dev store without creating a database, changing permissions, or
    /// running the writable store's initialization and upgrade path.
    pub fn open_read_only(path: &Path) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let mut conn = Connection::open_with_flags(path, rusqlite::OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_ONLY)?;
        #[cfg(unix)]
        let file_identity = file_identity(&conn)?;
        conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;
        let tx = conn.transaction()?;
        let version: String = tx.query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'version'", [], |row| {
                row.get(0)
            })?;
        if version != STORE_VERSION && version != dormant::VERSION && version != "snapback4-release-store-2" && version != backfills::VERSION { return Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("store version {version} is not {STORE_VERSION}"))); }
        let catalog = tx.prepare("SELECT name, num FROM _catalog")?.query_map([], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        let identity = activation::identity(&tx)?;
        tx.rollback()?;
        let real = std::fs::canonicalize(path).unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_path_buf());
        let assets_dir = real.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new(".")).join("assets");
        let page_size = conn.query_row("PRAGMA page_size", [], |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0))? as u64;
        Ok(Self { #[cfg(test)] full_adoption: false, adoption_report: Default::default(), conn,
            server: false, ownership: None, #[cfg(unix)] file_identity, page_size, catalog, identity, assets_dir, ephemeral_assets: false })
    }

    /// Classify against one read snapshot of the active generation and rows.
    pub fn successor(&mut self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<Vec<crate::successor::Step>, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let steps = successor::assess(&tx, schema)?;
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(steps)
    }

    /// Preview the same atomic launch admitted by dev, without changing the store.
    pub fn check_timeline_activation(&mut self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<Vec<timeline_launch::Estimate>, RuntimeError> {
        self.check_timeline_activation_at(schema, crate::now_ms(), true)
    }

    pub fn check_timeline_activation_at(&mut self, schema: &Schema, now: i64, allow_launch: bool) -> Result<Vec<timeline_launch::Estimate>, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let estimates = timeline_launch::preflight(&tx, schema, now, allow_launch)?;
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(estimates)
    }

    pub fn open(path: &Path) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::open_with_busy_timeout(path, std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
    }

    /// A caller with its own pre-admission deadline can own the lock wait.
    // @ref LLP 3000.011.004.000#2 — only open/adoption retries use a caller-owned deadline.
    pub fn open_with_busy_timeout(path: &Path, timeout: std::time::Duration) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::open_base(path, timeout, true)
    }

    // Shared server/device initialization; server evidence is added only by
    // the server entry points, never when opening an older native device.
    fn open_base(path: &Path, timeout: std::time::Duration, server: bool) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let io = |e: std::io::Error| RuntimeError::Store(format!("private store: {e}"));
        // Release links intentionally point at the one shared database.
        let real = std::fs::canonicalize(path).unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_path_buf());
        if let Some(parent) = real.parent().filter(|p| !p.as_os_str().is_empty()) {
            // A store's parent is its data root (assets live beside it), so
            // opening an existing store tightens that root as well.
            crate::private_fs::directory(parent).map_err(io)?;
        }
        // SQLite's Unix VFS copies the database mode when creating WAL,
        // journal and shared-memory files. Set it before opening SQLite;
        // chmod only after preparation would leave a disclosure window.
        crate::private_fs::database_file(&real).map_err(io)?;
        for suffix in ["-wal", "-shm", "-journal"] {
            crate::private_fs::existing_file(&PathBuf::from(format!("{}{suffix}", real.display())))
                .map_err(io)?;
        }
        let conn = Connection::open(&real)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.033#1.3 — even dropping a refused writable connection
        // must preserve committed WAL frames instead of checkpointing them.
        conn.set_db_config(rusqlite::config::DbConfig::SQLITE_DBCONFIG_NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE, true)?;
        conn.busy_timeout(timeout)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.013#4-following-without-caps — published watermarks must survive a power loss.
        // Delivery publishes partition watermarks as well as replayable copies.
        // Losing an acknowledged background commit could rewind a device's
        // watermark, so requests and maintenance share the durable policy.
        // A refused open must preserve even a legacy rollback-journal file's
        // header. New stores choose WAL; existing stores retain their mode.
        if conn.query_row("PRAGMA page_count", [], |r| r.get::<_, i64>(0))? == 0 {
            conn.execute_batch("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;")?;
        }
        conn.execute_batch("PRAGMA synchronous = FULL; PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF;")?;
        // A release serves the shared store through a link; the assets sit
        // beside the file itself, so every release serves the same bytes.
        let real = std::fs::canonicalize(path).unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_path_buf());
        let assets_dir = real.parent().map(|dir| dir.join("assets")).unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from("assets"));
        let store = Self::prepare(conn, assets_dir, false, server)?;
        for suffix in ["-wal", "-shm", "-journal"] {
            crate::private_fs::existing_file(&PathBuf::from(format!("{}{suffix}", real.display())))
                .map_err(io)?;
        }
        }
        Ok(store)
    }

    pub fn memory() -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        let assets_dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
            "snapback4-{}-{}", std::process::id(), crate::ids::mint(crate::now_ms(), &mut crate::ids::Mint::default())?
        ));
        Self::prepare(Connection::open_in_memory()?, assets_dir, true, true)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.023#12 — create server metadata at open, before reads can
    // cache plans. DDL during a commit would invalidate those prepared reads.
    fn initialize_change_evidence(conn: &Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        conn.execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _change_evidence (seq INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, footprint TEXT NOT NULL)")?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Where this store keeps uploaded bytes (`assets/<id>`).
    pub fn assets_dir(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.assets_dir
    }

    /// Whether the assets directory belongs to this store alone (a store in
    /// memory) rather than to the project beside the database.
    pub fn ephemeral_assets(&self) -> bool {
        self.ephemeral_assets
    }

    fn prepare(mut conn: Connection, assets_dir: PathBuf, ephemeral_assets: bool, server: bool) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        #[cfg(unix)]
        let file_identity = file_identity(&conn)?;
        // Our fixed indexed SQL should reuse its plan when keys or limits
        // change. Set this before caching statements: an existing VDBE
        // retains its parameter-expiry mask even after automatic reprepare.
        // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — reuse SQLite plans across bound inputs
        conn.set_db_config(rusqlite::config::DbConfig::SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_QPSG, true)?;
        // An ephemeral directory is created by the first upload (`Upload::write`)
        // and has no earlier contents to make private.
        if !ephemeral_assets {
            let io = |e: std::io::Error| RuntimeError::Store(format!("private assets: {e}"));
            crate::private_fs::directory(&assets_dir).map_err(io)?;
            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&assets_dir).map_err(io)? {
                crate::private_fs::existing_file(&entry.map_err(io)?.path()).map_err(io)?;
            }
        }
        // Catalog numbers and ownership must come from the same snapshot.
        // An activation between separate reads must never attach new IR to
        // the previous catalog, even when every number is monotone.
        let opened = conn.transaction_with_behavior(rusqlite::TransactionBehavior::Immediate)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.033#1.3 — inspect existing stores without upgrading
        // them. All upgrades join successful activation, never a refused open.
        let fresh: bool = opened.query_row("SELECT NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type = 'table')", [], |r| r.get(0))?;
        if fresh {
            Self::initialize_schema(&opened)?;
            if server { Self::initialize_change_evidence(&opened)?; }
        }
        let version: String = opened.query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'version'", [], |row| {
                row.get(0)
            })?;
        if version != STORE_VERSION && version != dormant::VERSION && version != "snapback4-release-store-2" && version != backfills::VERSION { return Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("store version {version} is not {STORE_VERSION}; this data directory belongs to another Snapback"))); }
        let catalog = opened.prepare("SELECT name, num FROM _catalog")?.query_map([], |row| {
                Ok((row.get::<_, String>(0)?, row.get::<_, i64>(1)?))
            })?.collect::<Result<BTreeMap<_, _>, _>>()?;
        let identity = activation::identity(&opened)?;
        opened.commit()?;
        let page_size = conn.query_row("PRAGMA page_size", [], |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0))? as u64;
        Ok(Self { #[cfg(test)] full_adoption: false, adoption_report: Default::default(), conn,
            server,
            ownership: None, #[cfg(unix)] file_identity, page_size, catalog, assets_dir, ephemeral_assets, identity })
    }

    pub(super) fn initialize_schema(conn: &Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        conn.execute_batch(
            "CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _meta (key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, value TEXT NOT NULL);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _catalog (name TEXT PRIMARY KEY, num INTEGER NOT NULL UNIQUE);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS r (t INTEGER NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, data TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (t, id)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS i (ti INTEGER NOT NULL, k BLOB NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (ti, k)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _commits (seq INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, at INTEGER NOT NULL, program TEXT NOT NULL, principal TEXT NOT NULL, kind TEXT NOT NULL, touched TEXT NOT NULL, writes INTEGER NOT NULL);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _log (partition TEXT NOT NULL, seq INTEGER NOT NULL, ord INTEGER NOT NULL, t TEXT NOT NULL, kind TEXT NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, data TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (partition, seq, ord)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             DROP INDEX IF EXISTS _log_row_seq;
             DROP INDEX IF EXISTS _log_row_seq_kind;
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _ops (principal TEXT NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, seq INTEGER NOT NULL, outcome TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (principal, id)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _principals (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, kind TEXT NOT NULL, name TEXT, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _sessions (token_digest TEXT PRIMARY KEY, principal TEXT NOT NULL, kind TEXT NOT NULL, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL, expires_at INTEGER NOT NULL);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _credentials (principal TEXT PRIMARY KEY, email TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE, hash TEXT NOT NULL);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _generations (generation INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, schema TEXT NOT NULL, programs TEXT NOT NULL, at INTEGER NOT NULL);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _outbox (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, seq INTEGER NOT NULL, effect TEXT NOT NULL, payload TEXT NOT NULL, state TEXT NOT NULL, attempts INTEGER NOT NULL, at INTEGER NOT NULL);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _effect_steps (
                 effect_id TEXT NOT NULL, name TEXT NOT NULL, input_digest TEXT NOT NULL,
                 provider_key TEXT NOT NULL, state TEXT NOT NULL, claim_digest TEXT NOT NULL,
                 result TEXT, at INTEGER NOT NULL, settled_at INTEGER, error TEXT,
                 PRIMARY KEY(effect_id, name));
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _effect_presence (principal TEXT NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, deadline INTEGER NOT NULL, reconnected INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, PRIMARY KEY (principal, id)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS _effect_presence_id ON _effect_presence(id);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _time_openings (at INTEGER NOT NULL, t TEXT NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY(t, id)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS _time_openings_due ON _time_openings(at, t, id);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _time_publications (t TEXT NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY(t, id)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _assets (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, owner TEXT NOT NULL, kind TEXT NOT NULL, content_type TEXT NOT NULL, bytes INTEGER NOT NULL, width INTEGER, height INTEGER, duration INTEGER, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL, home_table TEXT, home_id TEXT, home_column TEXT, released_at INTEGER, name TEXT);
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _asset_intents (owner TEXT NOT NULL, intent TEXT NOT NULL, fingerprint TEXT, asset TEXT, facts TEXT, placement TEXT, state TEXT NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY(owner, intent)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _asset_views (asset TEXT NOT NULL, principal TEXT NOT NULL, at INTEGER NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (asset, principal)) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _asset_gc (asset TEXT PRIMARY KEY) WITHOUT ROWID;
             CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _jobs (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, name TEXT NOT NULL, args TEXT NOT NULL, program_hash TEXT NOT NULL, state TEXT NOT NULL, sweep INTEGER NOT NULL, cursor TEXT, upper TEXT, chunks INTEGER NOT NULL, rows_written INTEGER NOT NULL, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL, updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL, error TEXT);",
        )?;
        conn.execute_batch("CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS _sessions_expiry ON _sessions(expires_at);")?;
        crate::identity::initialize(&conn)?;
        let job_columns: Vec<String> = conn.prepare("SELECT name FROM pragma_table_info('_jobs')")?.query_map([], |row| row.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        for column in ["chunk_size", "retries", "heap_peak"] {
            if !job_columns.contains(&column.to_owned()) { conn.execute_batch(&format!("ALTER TABLE _jobs ADD COLUMN {column} INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;"))?; }
        }
        if !job_columns.iter().any(|column| column == "domain") { conn.execute_batch("ALTER TABLE _jobs ADD COLUMN domain TEXT;")?; }
        // @ref LLP 3000.038#4 — a file's sanitized name rides the ledger; older stores gain the column in place.
        let asset_columns: Vec<String> = conn.prepare("SELECT name FROM pragma_table_info('_assets')")?.query_map([], |row| row.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        if !asset_columns.iter().any(|column| column == "name") { conn.execute_batch("ALTER TABLE _assets ADD COLUMN name TEXT;")?; }
        let intent_columns:Vec<String>=conn.prepare("SELECT name FROM pragma_table_info('_asset_intents')")?.query_map([],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        if !intent_columns.iter().any(|c|c=="placement"){conn.execute_batch("ALTER TABLE _asset_intents ADD COLUMN placement TEXT;")?;}
        let op_columns: Vec<String> = conn.prepare("SELECT name FROM pragma_table_info('_ops')")?.query_map([], |row| row.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        for (column, kind) in [("name", "TEXT"), ("args_digest", "TEXT"), ("at", "INTEGER")] {
            if !op_columns.iter().any(|name| name == column) {
                conn.execute_batch(&format!("ALTER TABLE _ops ADD COLUMN {column} {kind};"))?;
            }
        }
        // Discard payload columns from the pre-release binding implementation.
        for column in ["args", "result"] {
            if op_columns.iter().any(|name| name == column) {
                conn.execute_batch(&format!("ALTER TABLE _ops DROP COLUMN {column};"))?;
            }
        }
        crate::engine::actions::initialize(&conn)?;
        // The outbox grew a worker protocol (leases, backoff, results); a
        // store from before it gains the columns in place, once.
        let outbox_columns: Vec<String> = conn.prepare("SELECT name FROM pragma_table_info('_outbox')")?.query_map([], |row| row.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
        for (column, kind) in [("lease_until", "INTEGER"), ("claimed_by", "TEXT"), ("next_at", "INTEGER"), ("result", "TEXT"), ("error", "TEXT"), ("settled_at", "INTEGER"), ("work_spec", "TEXT"), ("deadline", "INTEGER"), ("release", "TEXT"), ("error_code", "TEXT"), ("lease_digest", "TEXT"), ("cancelled", "INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0"), ("contract", "TEXT")] {
            if !outbox_columns.iter().any(|name| name == column) {
                conn.execute_batch(&format!("ALTER TABLE _outbox ADD COLUMN {column} {kind};"))?;
            }
        }
        conn.execute_batch("CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS _outbox_due ON _outbox(state, effect, seq);")?;
        conn.execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _meta(key, value) VALUES ('version', ?1)", [STORE_VERSION])?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.2 — a database incarnation survives reopen, independently of schema generation.
        conn.execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _meta(key, value) VALUES ('store_id', lower(hex(randomblob(16))))", [])?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(crate) fn check_selected_release(conn: &Connection, expected: Option<u64>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if let Some(expected) = expected {
            let release: Option<String> = conn.query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'release'", [], |r| {
                    r.get(0)
                }).optional()?;
            if release.as_deref().and_then(|s| s.parse::<u64>().ok()) != Some(expected) {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the current release changed while starting; retry serve".into()));
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // Closing an accepted backend may checkpoint normally. Failure to restore
    // this optional close-time optimization leaves the conservative setting;
    // it must not turn a committed acceptance into a returned refusal.
    pub(crate) fn accept_open(&self) {
        let _ = self.conn.set_db_config(rusqlite::config::DbConfig::SQLITE_DBCONFIG_NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE, false);
    }

    pub fn transaction(&mut self) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
        self.check_ownership()?;
        // The write lock and identity check are one boundary: an engine
        // opened before another process activated a successor cannot write
        // or read with its stale schema/catalog.
        let tx = self.conn.transaction_with_behavior(rusqlite::TransactionBehavior::Immediate)?;
        if activation::identity(&tx)? != self.identity {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the store adopted another backend; reopen this engine".into()));
        }
        Ok(Tx { activation_scan: None, changes: Default::default(), sequence_table: Default::default(), tx, page_size: self.page_size,
            server: self.server,
            catalog: &self.catalog })
    }

    /// A consistent read snapshot does not reserve SQLite's writer lock.
    pub fn read_transaction(&mut self) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
        self.check_ownership()?;
        let tx = self.conn.transaction_with_behavior(rusqlite::TransactionBehavior::Deferred)?;
        if activation::identity(&tx)? != self.identity {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the store adopted another backend; reopen this engine".into()));
        }
        Ok(Tx { activation_scan: None, changes: Default::default(), sequence_table: Default::default(), tx, page_size: self.page_size,
            server: self.server,
            catalog: &self.catalog })
    }

    /// One deferred snapshot for an authority walk from an API that only
    /// borrows the engine. Writer operations pass their existing `Tx` instead.
    pub(crate) fn authority_transaction(&self) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
        self.check_ownership()?;
        let tx = self.conn.unchecked_transaction()?;
        if activation::identity(&tx)? != self.identity {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the store adopted another backend; reopen this engine".into()));
        }
        Ok(Tx { activation_scan: None, changes: Default::default(), sequence_table: Default::default(), tx, page_size: self.page_size,
            server: self.server,
            catalog: &self.catalog })
    }

    /// An independent read connection, bound to this catalog and identity.
    pub fn reader(&self) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        self.check_ownership()?;
        let path = self.conn.path().filter(|path| !path.is_empty())
            .ok_or_else(|| {
                RuntimeError::Store("an in-memory store has no independent reader".into())
            })?;
        let conn = Connection::open_with_flags(path, rusqlite::OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_ONLY)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — detached readers must answer from the owned database, not a replacement at its path.
        // Compare after opening, before reading. Stat only: closing another raw
        // database descriptor would discard this process's SQLite POSIX locks.
        #[cfg(unix)]
        if file_identity(&conn).ok().flatten() != self.file_identity {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("owned database at {path} moved or was replaced; stop and reopen its current path")));
        }
        // This connection skips prepare's schema writes but needs the same
        // planning policy before any statements are cached.
        // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — reuse SQLite plans across bound inputs
        conn.set_db_config(rusqlite::config::DbConfig::SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_QPSG, true)?;
