321:pub(crate) fn interpretation<'a>(schema: &'a Schema, table: &str, field: &str) -> Option<(Option<&'a ColumnType>, Option<&'a ColumnDefault>, Option<&'a crate::schema::RecordedField>)> {
//! Privileged, bounded population evidence for adoption refusals.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#3-older-writes-and-incompatible-values — count all affected rows, retain at most five samples.
use super::*;
use crate::schema::{Constraint, materialize_optional_fields};

#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct Samples {
    pub count: u64,
    ids: Vec<String>,
    values: Vec<String>,
    last_id: Option<String>,
}
pub(super) fn bounded(text: &str) -> String {
    if text.len() <= 256 {
        return text.into();
    }
    let mut end = 256;
    while !text.is_char_boundary(end) {
        end -= 1;
    }
    format!("{}… [truncated]", &text[..end])
}

fn value_sample(value: &Value) -> String {
    struct Prefix(Vec<u8>);
    impl std::io::Write for Prefix {
        fn write(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> std::io::Result<usize> {
            let n = bytes.len().min(256 - self.0.len());
            self.0.extend_from_slice(&bytes[..n]);
            if n < bytes.len() {
                return Err(std::io::Error::other("sample truncated"));
            }
            Ok(n)
        }
        fn flush(&mut self) -> std::io::Result<()> {
            Ok(())
        }
    }
    if let Value::Bytes(bytes) = value {
        return format!("{} bytes", bytes.len());
    }
    let mut prefix = Prefix(Vec::with_capacity(256));
    let truncated = serde_json::to_writer(&mut prefix, &crate::wire::Plain(value)).is_err();
    let mut text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&prefix.0).into_owned();
    if truncated {
        text.push_str("… [truncated]")
    }
    text
}
impl Samples {
    pub fn add(&mut self, id: &str, value: &str) {
        if self.last_id.as_deref() != Some(id) {
            self.count += 1;
            if self.ids.len() < 5 {
                self.ids.push(bounded(id));
            }
            self.last_id = Some(id.into());
        }
        let value = bounded(value);
        if self.values.len() < 5 && !self.values.contains(&value) {
            self.values.push(value);
        }
    }
    pub fn describe(&self) -> String {
        format!(
            "{} affected rows; sample ids [{}]; values/keys [{}]",
            self.count,
            self.ids.join(", "),
            self.values.join(", ")
        )
    }
}

pub(super) fn candidate(
    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    schema: &Schema,
    previous: Option<&Schema>,
    complete: bool,
) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for (name, table) in schema
        .tables
        .iter()
        .filter(|(n, t)| t.ephemeral.is_none() && schema.maintain_for(n).is_none())
    {
        if previous.and_then(|p| p.table(name)) == Some(table) {
            continue;
        }
        let mut bad = Samples::default();
        super::activation::walk(tx, tx.num(name)?, |id, mut row| {
            if let Value::Row(f) | Value::Map(f) = &mut row {
                f.retain(|c, _| table.columns.contains_key(c));
            }
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
            let validation = if complete {
                crate::engine::validate::validate_row(schema, name, &row)
            } else {
                crate::engine::validate::validate_adoption_input(schema, name, &row)
            };
            if let Err(error) = validation {
                let mut detailed = false;
                for (column, kind) in &table.columns {
                    if (complete || !table.engine_field(column))
                        && crate::engine::validate::validate_column(
                            name,
                            column,
                            kind,
                            row.field(column).ok(),
                        )
                        .is_err()
                    {
                        let value = row
                            .field(column)
                            .map(value_sample)
                            .unwrap_or_else(|_| "<missing>".into());
                        bad.add(id, &format!("{name}.{column}={value}; {error}"));
                        detailed = true;
                    }
                }
                if !detailed {
                    bad.add(id, &error.to_string());
                }
            }
            for (column, kind) in &table.columns {
                if let Err(error) =
                    super::activation::validate_assets(tx, kind, row.field(column).ok())
                {
                    bad.add(id, &format!("{name}.{column}: {error}"));
                }
            }
            Ok(())
        })?;
        if bad.count != 0 {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural {
                code: "E_ADOPT",
                message: format!(
                    "table {name}: {}; rewrite: repair the named values under the current release, or deploy a bridge with optional/literal-default columns using --restore-dormant, backfill through a bridge mutation, then deploy required types; inspect: snapback4 sql \"SELECT * FROM [{name}]\" --release <dir>",
                    bad.describe()
                ),
            });
        }
        for (index, definition) in table.indexes.iter().filter(|(_, i)| {
            i.unique
                && (complete
                    || !table
                        .columns
                        .keys()
                        .any(|c| table.engine_field(c) && i.uses_column(c)))
        }) {
            groups(
                tx,
                name,
                table,
                &format!("{name}.{index}"),
                definition.components.len(),
                1,
                true,
                |row| index_values(definition, row),
            )?;
        }
        for constraint in &table.constraints {
            if let Constraint::Cap { by, max } = constraint
                && (complete || !by.iter().any(|c| table.engine_field(c)))
            {
                groups(
                    tx,
                    name,
                    table,
                    &format!("{name} cap by {}", by.join(",")),
                    by.len(),
                    u64::from(*max),
                    table.cap_exempts_null(by),
                    |row| {
                        by.iter()
                            .map(|c| row.field(c).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null))
                            .collect()
                    },
                )?;
            }
        }
    }
# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```
For a complete real-user staging script and first-admin setup, run `guide staging`. Bulk account setup is restricted to fresh `deploy --staging` stores. Generations climb on every actual switch, including rollback and forward; selecting identical execution content is a no-op. `token --service ops --release ./deployment` works while serve runs. Signups are rate-limited: create proof accounts sequentially and respect a returned retry delay. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development and classified staging proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`); production always uses wall time.

## Upgrade
`snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes; commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. Use the password and first-admin recipes below.

## Deploy and password identity
Add `use identity password` (included below). Deploy from the app directory onto durable storage:
Serve admits bearer sessions; personas are 401 and /seed/dev doors 403, even through proxies. Signup creates an account, not a privileged role. See guide client for SDK session stores; auth/headless for account setup. HTTP auth returns session/denied; SDK signup/login from `snapback4/auth` returns ok/why. Check warns on installed version skew.
```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```
Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness.

Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.

## First admin: a service seat
Provision roles through an operator seat, with no public first-user race:
```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  sync
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```
```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
unset TOKEN
```
This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads.

A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.

## Migrations and adoption
Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or a declarative `backfill table name(fields: types): {column: expression}`; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility.

## Rollback after widening, restore and prune
```sh
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
```
`deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused candidate is never selectable. Bare rollback selects the previously active release; rollback prints the resolved target before preview or activation. Generation is a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Identical source is a no-op. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. A dev/older unreachable owner requires stopping that owned process before retrying; failed adoption keeps current.

Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; rollback selects restoration explicitly. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Rollback preview identifies current safety guards/rules and read confidentiality the target loses, active table-population changes, and current → target quotas. Preserve the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release, or pause that workflow. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.

## Bridge a safety gate before rollback
Use this beside the enum-widening rollback recipe above. Suppose v2 added `grounding.state = 'inspection'`; v1 holds only `'clear'`/`'hold'`. Selecting v1 hides that row and its children, so v1's `book` guard may allow a booking that v2 blocks. Counts and sample ids are snapshot dependencies, not predicate matches; later writes can change the plan.

1. Start from the old UI/operations, but keep v2's widened `grounding.state` domain, safety table, read audience and booking `require`. Save this complete source as `versions/bridge/schema.q` and deploy it with `deploy ./deployment --schema versions/bridge/schema.q --check`, then without `--check`.
2. While the gate still runs, migrate each inspection to a truthful old-domain hold through an authorized bridge mutation. Do not relabel it clear just to satisfy v1. Keep linked metadata until its parent can be safely represented; if v1 cannot invalidate newer metadata, keep that behavior in the bridge too.
3. Prove the same booking/payment still refuses for the blocked case and that the named lists/counts preserve the intended business denominator. Re-run the final old-source preview against the repaired live population.
4. Select v1 only when those checks pass. If the old source cannot preserve the invariant, keep the bridge or pause that workflow. Retained storage cannot supply absent logic.

## What the old release sees
| Request after rollback | Result |
|---|---|
| An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
| Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
| Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
| Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
| Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
| Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |

This proof assumes v1 declares `readItem(item)` returning `items[item]` and `editItem(item)` with `row = items[item]! else NOT_FOUND`; v2 adds `newOnly` and writes an enum value v1 cannot hold. Keep the bearer session in `headers`:
```js
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', '1'); const absent = await fetch(url + '/q/newOnly', {method:'POST', headers, body:'{"args":{}}'});
assert.equal(absent.status, 404); assert.equal((await absent.json()).denied.code, 'E_OP');
assert.equal((await post('/m/editItem', {item})).why.code, 'NOT_FOUND');
assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data, null);
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', 'latest'); assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data.status, 'new');
```
Previews lead with removed guards and their refusal codes, then group query/count active-table losses, linked dormant rows and safety dependencies per change. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Original read-through captures restore only with the same capture definition; new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence. Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.

## Clients across rollback
Save versions/v1/schema.q (and any other .q modules with their original names) outside snapback/ and create `snapback/client-compatibility.json` containing `{"baseline":"versions/v1","since_release":2}`. `check`/`dev` emit added row/result fields and existing-operation parameters as optional, labelled "Since release 2", retaining older result value domains. This is your release label, not server generation; ordinary types stay required without opt-in. Use `row.color ?? "none"` for the baseline fallback. Keep contemporary fixtures in versions/v1/fixtures/; deploy with `--schema versions/v1` and test with `--source versions/v1` (guide testing). Baselines resolve from the original project root, including saved-source deploys.

`useRelease()` from snapback4/react follows the authenticated catalog even when query gates are closed: loading, denied, or `{generation, operations, tables}` with public argument/field shapes. Generated `api.op.available(release)` is false while unready or when name/kind/argument signatures differ. This checks capability, not authorization or unchanged business semantics; still handle E_OP/E_INPUT and authored guard refusals when a switch races it. The SDK withdraws old metadata on observed generations, polls while subscribed, and returns loading offline/on catalog failure; account retirement/close withdraws it. Keep draft state above generation-sensitive read/action gates; remount only the gated children. Dormant means unavailable on this release, not deleted. Test v2 → v1 → v2 with one frontend: field fallbacks, missing operations, retained drafts and restored values. Alternatively check selected immutable source in a separate copy and rebuild its matching frontend. Preserve deployment source; Expo/Exact runtime changes also require a device rebuild.

## Trigger a deployed job
A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.
```sh
export SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
bunx snapback4 run reconcile --release ./deployment --json
unset SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN
```
`--token-env NAME` reads another environment variable. The command uses served source, so it also works outside the app directory. A lost reply is ambiguous; inspect the queue before retrying. Starter/client recipes: `guide client`.

## Persona transition checklist
The complete account directory, linked membership and real-account transition is `guide production`.
1. Add password identity and signup/login UI; persist sessions with localStorageSessionStore on web or the secure native store, and verify restart.
2. Replace hard-coded persona owners/UI with signed-in account principals; map roles through the trusted seat recipe.
3. Give profiles a production write path and authorized directory; expose no credentials.
4. Create required teams/memberships, invitations or principal sharing; wait for scope delivery before offline reads; test expiry, revocation and outsiders.
5. Bootstrap every required table without fixtures/seed; guide start/client covers first writers and password starters.
6. Prove real-account writes, role checks, sign-out and rollback on durable storage behind TLS.

## Inspection, proxies and TLS
| Door | `dev` | `serve` |
|---|---|---|
| `/q`, `/m`, `GET /` compatibility record | Loopback persona or bearer | Bearer session required, including public rows |
| `/auth/config`; signup/login/guest | Public config; declared sign-in methods | Public config; declared sign-in methods |
| `/seed`, `/dev/*` | Development only | Refused |
| `/jobs/<job>` | Service seat | Full service seat |

Public visitors obtain guest sessions with `use identity`; `use identity password` disables guests (`guide rule-recipes`).

`snapback4 sql 'SELECT id, archived FROM notes' --release ./deployment --json` returns positional rows under `columns`; `--json-objects` returns object rows with direct boolean columns as true/false. Schema-qualified relations, recursive or shadowing/forward-reference CTEs need positional --json. Inspection bypasses read rules and can show expired stored rows; it is an operator tool, not an app query surface. Bind loopback behind a TLS proxy. Forward Authorization and preserve response bodies; allow long polls without buffering or early timeouts. Non-loopback HTTP requires `--i-understand-cleartext` and a trusted private hop behind TLS. Identity rate keys use the socket peer, not forwarded headers. Serve refuses personas and dev doors even through a local proxy; see SERVER.md for capacity. `--json-objects` returns `{header, columns, rows, limit, more, ruleFiltered:false}` with object `rows`; `--json` uses positional `rows`. Put flags outside the quoted SQL.

## Authoring and proof refusals
N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER means no production insert path can bootstrap this table. It groups root blockers with their dependents; persona-only schemas get one identity note. Add a service-seat insert mutation and provision real account relationships. E_UPGRADE means installation or post-upgrade check failed. Repair the package-manager or registry error and rerun upgrade. Packages may already be pinned: if check failed, repair the reported schema/device refusal and rerun check.

## Capture a saved version
After check and business cases pass, from the app root:
```sh
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --save-version versions/v1
snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check --bridge ./bridge
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --schema ./bridge --check
```

The new versions/v1 captures selected modules, their contemporary fixtures and `versions/v1/release.json` with the actual immutable release id, source hash and CLI version. Unchanged deploy keeps its id; existing snapshots never change. Choose a new positive-numbered versions/vN; saving cannot combine with --check. Saved-source deploy uses its own fixtures. Use the recorded id when choosing a baseline; since_release remains the author-supplied newer-schema label. The bridge starts from complete current Quarry source and this app's `bridge/ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md`. Retain current domains, audiences, guards and quotas; repair records through authorized mutations; prove blocked cases refuse and affected lists/counts remain correct; repeat the old-source preview. No bridge is activated or business migration synthesized. JSON includes checklist; --verbose retains full evidence/index paths. Dormant rows reserve ids but no unique slots: new active rows may block restoration. A widened non-unique bridge permits repair before reinstating uniqueness; retained storage cannot supply absent safety logic.

Replacing an earlier transform first restores its reversible preimages; declare historical input types covering those originals and any native rows from the newer release.

Rows created under a backfilled release keep their authored outputs when the same declaration returns with unchanged inputs. If older code changes those inputs during rollback, re-entry refuses rather than overwriting the authored values; choose an explicit bridge or a new named transform to resolve the intended result.

## Persistent production: v1 → v2 → rollback → forward

Save the following as `day-two-production.mjs` in an app with `snapback4`
installed and run `node day-two-production.mjs`. `PRODUCTION_DIR` selects its
persistent deployment (default `./production-day-two`). Run it again against
the same directory: it preserves every existing ticket and adds two real-user
tickets. It never deletes production storage or reseeds fixtures. Keep the
deployment, logs and `versions/` outside source control as appropriate.

This example expands with a derived required field and contracts reviewer
reads to the owner, with an independent read invariant. An old-shaped write
still works on v2. Rollback deliberately restores v1's broader privacy rule;
assert that behavior, and use a privacy-preserving bridge when it is unsuitable.
REL5 owns release reactivation and old-client compatibility reporting; SEC3 owns
column privacy invariants and audit. Backfills transform stored data, not the
business rules an older release lacks.

```js
// day-two-production.mjs — this exact fence runs against the packed package.
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { resolve } from "node:path";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { cli, deploy, rollback, startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
import { signup } from "snapback4/auth";

const directory = resolve(process.env.PRODUCTION_DIR ?? "production-day-two");
const v1 = `use identity password
table roles:
  principal: principal
  role: enum('member', 'reviewer')
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  online only
mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('member', 'reviewer')):
  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return upsert roles[principal] {principal, role}
table tickets:
  owner: principal
  body: text 1..200
  weight: int
  length: int
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'reviewer']
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- .owner = viewer
  online only
mutation add(body: text 1..200, weight: int, length: int):
  return insert tickets {owner:viewer, body, weight, length}
mutation revise(ticket: tickets, weight: int):
  update tickets[ticket] {weight}
query ticket(ticket: tickets):
  return tickets[ticket]
`;
const v2 = v1
  .replace("  owner: principal", "  vehicleClass: enum('car', 'van', 'commercial')\n  owner: principal")
  .replace("  read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'reviewer']", "  read <- .owner = viewer")
  .replace("{owner:viewer, body, weight, length}", "{owner:viewer, body, weight, length, vehicleClass:'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car'}")
  + `backfill tickets vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  {vehicleClass:'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car'}
invariant tickets.read only .owner = viewer
`;
for (const [version, source] of [["v1", v1], ["v2", v2]]) {
  mkdirSync(`versions/${version}`, { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(`versions/${version}/schema.q`, source);
}
async function checked(args) {
  const result = await cli(args);
  assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr + result.stdout);
  return result.stdout;
}
async function snapshot() {
  const count = JSON.parse(await checked(["sql", "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets", "--release", directory, "--json"])).rows[0][0];
  const full = JSON.parse(await checked(["sql", "SELECT id, owner, body, weight, length FROM tickets ORDER BY id", "--release", directory, "--limit", String(Math.max(1, count + 1)), "--json"]));
  assert.equal(full.rows.length, count, "inspect the entire production population");
  return new Map(full.rows.map(row => [row[0], row]));
}
if (existsSync(directory + "/store.sqlite")) await rollback(directory, 1);
else await deploy(directory, "versions/v1");
const host = await startServe(directory);
try {
  const before = await snapshot();
  const users = [];
  for (const role of ["member", "reviewer"]) {
    const signed = await signup(host.url, `${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, randomUUID() + randomUUID());
    assert.equal(signed.ok, true);
    users.push(signed.session);
  }
  const [alice, reviewer] = users;
  async function post(path, session, args) {
    const response = await fetch(host.url + path, { method:"POST", headers:{"content-type":"application/json", authorization:`Bearer ${session.token}`}, body:JSON.stringify({args}), signal:AbortSignal.timeout(10000) });
    assert.equal(response.status, 200);
    return response.json();
  }
  const operator = {token:await host.operatorToken()};
  for (const [index, role] of ["member", "reviewer"].entries()) {
    assert.equal((await post("/m/provision", operator, {principal:users[index].principal, role})).state, "sent");
  }
  const first = await post("/m/add", alice, {body:"historical ticket", weight:1000, length:400});
  assert.equal(first.state, "sent");
  const ticket = first.result.id;
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data.body, "historical ticket", "v1 reviewer can read");
  const historical = await snapshot(); assert.equal(historical.size, before.size + 1);
  const preview = await checked(["deploy", directory, "--schema", "versions/v2", "--check", "--restore-dormant"]);
  assert.match(preview, /vehicleClass/);
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), historical, "preview changes no production rows");
  await deploy(directory, "versions/v2", {args:["--restore-dormant"]});
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), historical, "v2 preserves every historical business field");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "car");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "v2 tightened privacy");
  const oldCall = await post("/m/add", alice, {body:"old-shaped call on v2", weight:4000, length:400});
  assert.equal(oldCall.state, "sent"); assert.equal(oldCall.result.vehicleClass, "commercial");
  const allV2 = await snapshot(); assert.equal(allV2.size, historical.size + 1);
  await rollback(directory, 1);
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), allV2, "rollback preserves v2-native rows too");
  const oldView = (await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data;
  assert.equal(oldView.body, "historical ticket"); assert.ok(!("vehicleClass" in oldView));
  assert.equal((await post("/m/revise", alice, {ticket, weight:4000})).state, "sent");
  const changed = await snapshot();
  const expected = new Map(allV2); const changedRow = [...expected.get(ticket)]; changedRow[3] = 4000; expected.set(ticket, changedRow);
  assert.deepEqual(changed, expected, "v1 edits only the intended business value");
  await rollback(directory, "latest");
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), changed, "forward preserves the complete production population");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "commercial", "forward catches up the old-release edit");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "forward restores tightened privacy");
  console.log(JSON.stringify({status:"passed", persistent:true, before:before.size, after:changed.size, backfill:"car → commercial after v1 edit", privacy:"v1 broad → v2 private → v1 broad → v2 private"}));
} finally { await host.stop(); }
```

The deployment remains ready for `serve`. Every real user and existing row
survives. A business-state conflict reports the affected rows and a bridge
repair; resolve it under truthful old semantics before selecting that source.

exec

     1	//! Operator preflight runs the real activation on a private disk-backed snapshot.
     2	// REL3 owns dormant-visibility counts here; SURF clause narration lives in release_narration.
     3	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#92-exact-preview-and-operator-vocabulary — no live pointer, generation or audit changes.
     4	use super::*;
     5	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     6	struct Snapshot(PathBuf);
     7	impl Drop for Snapshot {
     8	    fn drop(&mut self) {
     9	        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
    10	    }
    11	}
    12	
    13	pub(super) fn preview(
    14	    store_path: &Path,
    15	    root: PathBuf,
    16	    release: u64,
    17	    options: &Options,
    18	) -> Result<(), String> {
    19	    let bound = super::releases::bound_at(root.clone())?;
    20	    let project = Project { root };
    21	    let native = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    22	    crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project,&bound.schema)?;
    23	    let id = snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default())
    24	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    25	    let snapshot = Snapshot(std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("snapback4-release-preview-{id}")));
    26	    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&snapshot.0).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    27	    let path = snapshot.0.join("store.sqlite");
    28	    // Probe the existing filesystem lock, independently of any HTTP address
    29	    // hint. Keep a stopped store excluded through preview; never create a lock
    30	    // sidecar or mutate its address record just to inspect ownership.
    31	    let (live,_stopped_guard)=if store_path.exists() {
    32	        let database=store_path.canonicalize().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    33	        match snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(&database,&snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_path(&database),false,0) {
    34	            Ok(owner)=>match owner.try_lock() {
    35	                Ok(())=>(false,Some(owner)),
    36	                Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock)=>(true,None),
    37	                Err(std::fs::TryLockError::Error(e))=>return Err(format!("preview owner probe failed: {e}")),
    38	            },
    39	            Err(e) if e.kind()==std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound=>(false,None),
    40	            Err(e)=>return Err(format!("preview owner probe failed: {e}")),
    41	        }
    42	    }else{(false,None)};
    43	    // SQLite VACUUM INTO takes a coherent read snapshot even with a live WAL writer.
    44	    // Busy/disk errors refuse this preview; never fall back to copying database bytes.
    45	    if store_path.exists() {
    46	    let source = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(store_path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    47	    source
    48	        .conn()
    49	        .execute("VACUUM INTO ?1", [path.to_string_lossy().as_ref()])
    50	        .map_err(|e| {
    51	            format!("snapshot preview failed (retry after store/disk becomes available): {e}")
    52	        })?;
    53	    drop(source);
    54	    }
    55	    let mut store = snapback4_core::Store::open(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    56	    let held = store
    57	        .release_status(0, None, None)
    58	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    59	    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — preview uses the owning host's authoritative time, without minting or advancing anything.
    60	    let activation_now=if live {
    61	        let status=crate::release_control::request(store_path.parent().ok_or("preview store has no deployment directory")?,crate::release_control::Command::Status{after:0,items_after:None,event:None})?;
    62	        if status["revision"]!=held["revision"] {return Err("preview raced release activation; retry against the current store".into());}
    63	        status["clock"]["now"].as_i64().ok_or("serving owner does not expose its activation clock; restart it with this CLI before preview")?
    64	    }else{snapback4_core::now_ms()};
    65	    let mut activation =
    66	        snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(release, held["release"].as_u64());
    67	    activation.expected_revision = held["revision"].as_u64();
    68	    activation.native_digest = native.installed.map(|n| n.digest);
    69	    activation.operation = "preview".into();
    70	    activation.restore_dormant = !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") || options.flags.contains_key("restore-dormant");
    71	    let mut steps = Vec::new();
    72	    let estimate = store
    73	        .adoption_estimate(&bound.schema)
    74	        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
    75	        .map(|e| e.summary());
    76	    let candidate_schema=bound.schema.clone();
    77	    let candidate_programs:std::collections::BTreeMap<_,_>=bound.programs.iter().map(|p|(p.program.name.clone(),p.program.clone())).collect();
    78	    let mut hazards=Vec::new();
    79	    let mut notes=Vec::new();
    80	    let prior:Option<(String,String)>=store.conn().query_row("SELECT schema,programs FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')",[],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?))).ok();
    81	    let prior_schema=prior.as_ref().map(|(schema,_)|serde_json::from_str::<snapback4_core::Schema>(schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())).transpose()?;
    82	    if let Some((schema,programs))=prior {
    83	        let prior:snapback4_core::Schema=serde_json::from_str(&schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    84	        steps=snapback4_core::successor::classify(&prior,&candidate_schema);
    85	        let programs:std::collections::BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::Program>=serde_json::from_str(&programs).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    86	        let target_label = format!("release {release}");
    87	        let compatibility = super::release_narration::compatibility(&store, &prior, &candidate_schema, held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0), &target_label, options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction"))?;
    88	        for line in compatibility {
    89	            if let Some(warning) = line.strip_prefix("WARNING ") { hazards.push(warning.to_owned()); }
    90	            else { notes.push(line.trim_start_matches("note: ").to_owned()); }
    91	        }
    92	        let rollback=!options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction");
    93	        let (behavior_warnings, behavior_notes) = super::release_narration::behavior(store_path, &store, &prior, &candidate_schema, &programs, &candidate_programs, &project, held["release"].as_u64(), &target_label, rollback)?;
    94	        hazards.extend(behavior_warnings); notes.extend(behavior_notes);
    95	        for name in prior.tables.keys().filter(|name|!candidate_schema.tables.contains_key(*name) && prior.maintain_for(name).is_none() && prior.tables[*name].ephemeral.is_none()) {
    96	            let count:u64=store.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r WHERE t=(SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1)",[name],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    97	            if count>0 {hazards.push(format!("table {name}: {count} rows become dormant; {target_label} lacks this table's behavior; preserve it in a bridge or accept its absence"));}
    98	        }
    99	    }
   100	    super::release_safeguards::capture(&store)?;
   101	    let mut engine = snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared_at(
   102	        store,
   103	        bound.schema,
   104	        bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
   105	        &activation,
   106	        options.flags.contains_key("allow-feed-adoption"),
   107	        activation_now,
   108	    )
   109	    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
   110	    let mut planned = engine
   111	        .store_mut()

   142	            format!("no standalone {unit} become dormant (cells of any dormant rows are counted with those rows)")
   143	        } else if count == 0 {
   144	            format!("no {unit} become dormant")
   145	        } else {
   146	            format!("its {unit} become dormant ({count} {unit}; restored on forward)")
   147	        };
   148	        step.message=step.message.replace("its rows are lost", &retained).replace("its values are lost", &retained);
   149	    }

   158	    } else {
   159	        println!("preview: release {release} would be current as generation {}; live release unchanged", engine.generation());

   161	        if !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") { println!("  checklist: prove the named refusal cases and affected lists/counts as real accounts before release {release}; `--bridge <new-dir>` saves current source plus this app's ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md"); }
   162	        println!("  storage: {}",snapback4_core::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport::dormant(&items));
   163	        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
   164	            for hazard in hazards { println!("  detail: WARNING {hazard}"); }
   165	            for note in notes { println!("  detail: note: {note}"); }

   167	            for item in &items { println!("  {}: {} {} dormant (restored on compatible forward); sample ids [{}]", item.key, item.rows, item.unit(), item.sample_ids.join(", ")); }
   168	            println!("{}", engine.store().adoption_report.summary());
   169	            if let Some(estimate) = estimate {
   170	                println!("{estimate}");
   171	            }
   172	        }
   173	    }
   174	    Ok(())
   175	}

fn column_change(old: &ColumnType, new: &ColumnType) -> Change {
    let list = |ty: &ColumnType| match ty {
        ColumnType::List { max, .. } => Some((0, *max)),
        ColumnType::ListRange { min, max, .. } => Some((*min, *max)),
        _ => None,
    };
    if let (Some((amin, amax)), Some((bmin, bmax))) = (list(old), list(new)) {
        let (ColumnType::List { of: a, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of: a, .. },
            ColumnType::List { of: b, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of: b, .. }) = (old, new) else { unreachable!() };
        let items_widen = a == b || column_change(a, b) == Change::Additive;
        return if items_widen && bmin <= amin && bmax >= amax { Change::Additive } else { Change::Narrowing };
    }
    match (old, new) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(a), ColumnType::Optional(b)) => column_change(a, b),
        // Optional becoming required narrows; required becoming optional widens.
        (ColumnType::Optional(a), b) => worse(Change::Narrowing, column_change(a, b)),
        (a, ColumnType::Optional(b)) => worse(Change::Additive, column_change(a, b)),
        (ColumnType::Text { max: a }, ColumnType::Text { max: b }) | (ColumnType::Bytes { max: a }, ColumnType::Bytes { max: b }) | (ColumnType::Json { max_bytes: a }, ColumnType::Json { max_bytes: b }) | (ColumnType::Image { max:a,.. },ColumnType::Image {max:b,..}) | (ColumnType::Video { max:a },ColumnType::Video {max:b}) => {
            if b >= a { Change::Additive } else { Change::Narrowing }
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#92-exact-preview-and-operator-vocabulary — widening asset bounds/allow-lists has the same rollback advisory as enum/text widening.
        (ColumnType::File {max:a,types:at},ColumnType::File {max:b,types:bt})=>{
            if b>=a && crate::assets::FILE_TYPES.iter().all(|t|!crate::assets::file_accepts(at,t) || crate::assets::file_accepts(bt,t)) {Change::Additive} else {Change::Narrowing}
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.040#16-schema-evolution — a wider int range applies in place; a tighter one is checked against every stored row.
        (ColumnType::IntRange { .. }, ColumnType::Int) => Change::Additive,
        (ColumnType::Int, ColumnType::IntRange { .. }) => Change::Narrowing,
        (ColumnType::IntRange { min: amin, max: amax }, ColumnType::IntRange { min: bmin, max: bmax }) => {
            if bmin <= amin && bmax >= amax { Change::Additive } else { Change::Narrowing }
        }
        (ColumnType::Enum { variants: a }, ColumnType::Enum { variants: b }) => {
            if a.iter().all(|v| b.contains(v)) { Change::Additive } else { Change::Narrowing }
        }
        _ => Change::Destructive,
    }
}

        Change::Additive => 0,
        Change::RuleChange => 1,

pub fn serve_release(options: &Options) -> Result<(), String> {
    let requested_port = crate::port_flag::parse("serve", options.flags.get("port").map(String::as_str))?;
    let bind = release_bind(options)?;
    let capacity = crate::host_limits::Capacity::from_options(&options.flags, |name| {
        std::env::var(name).ok()
    })?;
    let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
    crate::host_limits::initialize();
    let owner = crate::store_owner::host(&dir.join("store.sqlite"))?;
    owner.report_reclaim(options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
    let clock_offset = options.flags.get("clock-offset").map(|text| crate::dev_clock::offset(text).map_err(|e| e.to_string())).transpose()?.unwrap_or(0);
    if options.flags.contains_key("clock-offset") {
        let store = owner.open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        if store.meta("deployment_environment").map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() != Some("staging") { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release().to_string()); }
    }
    let current = current(&dir, &owner)?.ok_or("no current release; deploy first")?;
    let release = Project {
        root: dir.join("releases").join(current.to_string())
            .canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
    };
    crate::release_files::link_store(&release.root)?;
    let (port, listener) = crate::port_flag::listen("serve", requested_port, bind, None)?;
    let worker = options
        .flags
        .get("worker")
        .map(|path| {
            Path::new(path)
                .canonicalize()
                .map_err(|e| format!("--worker {path}: {e}"))
        })
        .transpose()?;
    crate::serve::serve(
        release,
        port,
        crate::serve::Mode::Release {
            clock_offset,
            expected: current,
            worker,
            owner: std::sync::Arc::new(owner),
        },
        &bind.to_string(),
        listener,

//! The successor under the stored rows: checked narrowing is additive when
//! the data already fits. Used by both read-only check and atomic activation.
use super::*;
use crate::schema::{ColumnDefault, Constraint, materialize_optional_fields};
use crate::successor::{Change, Step};
use crate::engine::validate::{validate_column, validate_row};

pub(super) fn assess(tx: &Tx<'_>, schema: &Schema) -> Result<Vec<Step>, RuntimeError> {
    let previous: Option<String> = tx.tx.query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation = (SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key = 'generation')", [], |row| row.get(0)).optional()?;
    let Some(previous) = previous else { return Ok(Vec::new()) };
    let previous: Schema = serde_json::from_str(&previous).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::decode(e, RuntimeError::Store))?;
    let mut steps = crate::successor::classify(&previous, schema);
    let mut count_checks = BTreeMap::<String, bool>::new();
    for step in &mut steps {
        if let Some(position) = step.path.strip_prefix("maintains[").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(']')).and_then(|s| s.parse::<usize>().ok()) {
            let maintain = &schema.maintains[position];
            // Existing activation is atomic and exhaustive. Keep its assessment
            // read-only and use the same interpreted contribution, without a write.
            if previous.tables.contains_key(&maintain.source) {
                let source = &schema.tables[&maintain.source];
                // @ref LLP 3000.000#implementation-follow-up — population proof shares validation, not sums.
                // A count's largest possible group is the entire source. Below
                // I53_MAX no predicate/key can overflow it. Validate the same
                // interpreted/captured source once for counts sharing a table;
                // sums retain their wide per-group cancellation assessment.
                let count_safe = matches!(maintain.kind, crate::schema::MaintainKind::Count)
                    && tx.tx.query_row("SELECT count(*) FROM r WHERE t = ?1", [tx.num(&maintain.source)?], |r| r.get::<_, i64>(0))? <= crate::value::I53_MAX;
                if count_safe && let Some(fits) = count_checks.get(&maintain.source) {
                    assessed(step, maintain, *fits);
                    continue;
                }
                let paths: Vec<_> = source.recorded.keys().filter(|path| !previous.tables[&maintain.source].recorded.contains_key(*path)).cloned().collect();
                let mut capture = crate::engine::runner::Runner::new(tx, schema, crate::engine::Context::system().at(crate::now_ms()), crate::meter::Meter::new(crate::meter::Ceilings::unbounded(), crate::meter::Cut::Abort), 0);
                let mut totals = BTreeMap::<Vec<u8>, i128>::new();
                let mut fits = true;
                super::activation::walk(tx, tx.num(&maintain.source)?, |_, mut row| {
                    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row { fields.retain(|field, _| source.columns.contains_key(field)); }
                    // Assess the same activation-time capture that the atomic
                    // migration will write; grouping uncaptured paths as null
                    // could falsely reject sums that fit their real groups.
                    let paths:Vec<_>=paths.iter().filter(|path|row.field(path).is_err()).cloned().collect();
                    let row = capture.capture_recorded(source, crate::schema::row_image(&row, source).into_owned(), &paths)?;
                    if validate_row(schema, &maintain.source, &row).is_err() { fits = false; return Ok(()); }
                    if !count_safe && maintain.selected(&row) {
                        let value = match &maintain.kind {
                            crate::schema::MaintainKind::Count => 1,
                            crate::schema::MaintainKind::Sum { of } => i128::from(row.field(of)?.as_int()?),
                        };
                        let total = totals.entry(crate::key::encode(&maintain.source_key(&row))).or_default();
                        match total.checked_add(value) { Some(next) => *total = next, None => fits = false }
                    }
                    Ok(())
                })?;
                fits &= totals.values().all(|total| (crate::value::I53_MIN as i128..=crate::value::I53_MAX as i128).contains(total));
                if count_safe { count_checks.insert(maintain.source.clone(), fits); }
                assessed(step, maintain, fits);
            }
            continue;
        }
        let path: Vec<_> = step.path.split('.').collect();
        let Some(name) = path.get(1).copied() else { continue };
        let Some(table) = schema.table(name) else { continue };
        // @ref LLP 3000.016#21a-owned-events-follow-their-source-rows — check previews the engine fill, never an unfilled bit as absence.
        // Actual activation fills before this assessment and still validates
        // every row. A read-only check cannot write the old store's images.
        if let ["tables", _, "columns", column] = path.as_slice()
            && !previous.tables[name].columns.contains_key(*column)
            && table.columns.get(*column) == Some(&crate::schema::ColumnType::Bool)
            && schema.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.posts[0] == name && t.slot(false) == *column) {
            step.change = Change::Additive;
            step.message = format!("event slot {name}.{column} is added; activation fills it from byActorPost before validation; devices reacquire");
            continue;
        }
        let column_step = matches!(path.as_slice(), ["tables", _, "columns", column] if previous.tables[name].columns.contains_key(*column));
        if step.change != Change::Narrowing && !(column_step && step.change == Change::Additive) { continue; }
        let t = tx.num(name)?;
        let populated: bool = tx.tx.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE t = ?1)", [t], |r| r.get(0))?;
        if !populated {
            step.change = Change::Additive;
            step.message = format!("{}; the table is empty", step.message.split(';').next().unwrap_or(&step.message));
            continue;
        }
        if matches!(path.as_slice(), ["tables", _, "sequences", _]) { continue; }
        if let ["tables", _, "columns", column] = path.as_slice() {
            if !previous.tables[name].columns.contains_key(*column) && matches!(table.defaults.get(*column), Some(ColumnDefault::Now | ColumnDefault::Viewer)) {
                continue;
            }
        }
        let mut fits = true;
        // Only the changed invariants need grouping; scan bounded image
        // buffers and retain a count per prospective key, never all rows.
        let mut groups: BTreeMap<(String, Vec<u8>), u64> = BTreeMap::new();
        super::activation::walk(tx, t, |_, mut row| {
            if !fits { return Ok(()); }
            if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row { fields.retain(|column, _| table.columns.contains_key(column)); }
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
            match path.as_slice() {
                ["tables", _, "columns", column] => {
                    let kind = &table.columns[*column];
                    fits = validate_column(name, column, kind, row.field(column).ok()).is_ok()
                        && super::activation::validate_assets(tx, kind, row.field(column).ok()).is_ok();
                    // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.3 — changing interpretation rechecks existing dependent invariants at the column's site.
                    for (index_name, index) in table.indexes.iter().filter(|(_, index)| index.unique && index.uses_column(column)) {
                        let values = index_values(index, &row);
                        if !values.contains(&Value::Null) {
                            let count = groups.entry((format!("unique:{index_name}"), key::encode(&values))).or_default();
                            *count += 1;
                            fits &= *count <= 1;
                        }
                    }
                    for constraint in &table.constraints {
                        match constraint {
                            Constraint::Cap { by, max } if by.iter().any(|c| c == column) => {
                                let values: Vec<_> = by.iter().map(|c| row.field(c).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null)).collect();
                                if table.cap_exempts_null(by) && values.contains(&Value::Null) {continue;}
                                let count = groups.entry((format!("cap:{}", canonical_json(by)), key::encode(&values))).or_default();
                                *count += 1;
                                fits &= *count <= u64::from(*max);
                            }
                            Constraint::Text { field, min, format } if field == column => {
                                if let Ok(Value::String(text)) = row.field(column) {
                                    fits &= crate::engine::validate::validate_text(&format!("{name}.{column}"), text, *min, *format).is_ok();
                                }
                            }
                            _ => {}
                        }
                    }
                }
                ["tables", _, "indexes", index] => {
                    let values = index_values(&table.indexes[*index], &row);
                    if !values.contains(&Value::Null) {
                        let count = groups.entry(((*index).into(), key::encode(&values))).or_default();
                        *count += 1;
                        fits = *count <= 1;
                    }
                }
                ["tables", _, "constraints"] => {
                    fits = validate_row(schema, name, &row).is_ok();
                    for constraint in &table.constraints {
                        if let Constraint::Cap { by, max } = constraint {
                            let values: Vec<_> = by.iter().map(|c| row.field(c).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null)).collect();
                                if table.cap_exempts_null(by) && values.contains(&Value::Null) {continue;}
                            let count = groups.entry((canonical_json(by), key::encode(&values))).or_default();
                            *count += 1;
                            fits &= *count <= u64::from(*max);
                        }
                    }
                }
                _ => {}
            }
//! Typed admission of rows and arguments at the store's trust boundary. A
//! value that does not fit its declared type is refused before any rule or
//! constraint sees it.

use crate::error::RuntimeError;
use crate::ir::{ArgType, Program};
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, Constraint, Schema, TextFormat, contains_system_principal};
use crate::value::{I53_MAX, I53_MIN, Value, canonical_json};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

pub fn validate_row(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, false, false)
}

/// Adoption builders still own recorded/captured slots. Check authoritative
/// fields before their work, and validate the complete image afterward.
#[cfg(feature="sqlite")]
pub(crate) fn validate_adoption_input(schema:&Schema,name:&str,row:&Value)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    let table=&schema.tables[name];
    for (column,kind) in &table.columns {
        if !table.engine_field(column) {validate_column(name,column,kind,row.field(column).ok())?;}
    }
    for constraint in &table.constraints {
        if let Constraint::Text{field,min,format}=constraint && !table.engine_field(field) && let Ok(Value::String(text))=row.field(field){validate_text(&format!("{name}.{field}"),text,*min,*format)?;}
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — row-view storage is a private numerical cache, not a disclosure.
// No certificate yet means no computed fields; public/input validation stays strict.
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub(crate) fn validate_maintained_cache(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let cache = schema.maintain_for(table_name).is_some_and(|m| m.row_key.is_some());
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, false, cache)
}

/// A predicting patch preserves unknown, untouched fields as omissions.
/// The authoritative writer always uses the complete-row validator.
pub(super) fn validate_projection(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value, viewer: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, viewer, false, false)
}

/// Application input is checked before engine slots are prepared. The final
/// stored image must also pass validate_row, with every required slot present.
pub(super) fn validate_before_slots(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, true, false)
}

fn validate_image(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value, viewer: Option<&str>, before_slots: bool, cache: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let table = schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
    let object = row.as_object()?;
    if !object.contains_key("id") {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name} row lacks id")));
    }
    if let Some(name) = object.keys().find(|name| !table.columns.contains_key(*name)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} row contains undeclared field {name}")));
    }
    for (name, kind) in &table.columns {
        if before_slots && crate::schema::timeline::is_slot(name) { continue; }
        if cache && matches!(name.as_str(), "value" | "recountPending") && !object.contains_key(name) { continue; }
        let value = object.get(name).or_else(|| table.maintained.contains_key(name).then_some(&Value::Int(0)));
        if value.is_none() && viewer.is_some_and(|viewer| table.written_by.get(name).is_some_and(|authority|
            !authority.audience && row.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(viewer.into())))) { continue; }
        validate_column(table_name, name, kind, value)?;
    }
    for constraint in &table.constraints {
        if let Constraint::Text { field, min, format } = constraint
            && let Some(Value::String(text)) = object.get(field)
        {
            validate_text(&format!("{table_name}.{field}"), text, *min, *format).map_err(|error| RuntimeError::Constraint(error.to_string().trim_start_matches("E_INPUT: ").to_owned()))?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub fn validate_column(table_name: &str, name: &str, kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let label = format!("{table_name}.{name}");
    if let Some(value) = value {
        crate::value::validate_identities(value)?;
        if contains_system_principal(value) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} cannot carry the reserved system principal")));
        }
    }
    if let Some(code_point) = disallowed_control(kind, value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} contains disallowed control character U+{code_point:04X}")));
    }
    validate_column_minimum(kind, value, &label)?;
    if !fits(kind, value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(match value {
            None | Some(Value::Null) => format!("{label} is required ({kind})"),
            Some(value) => format!("{label} must be {kind}, not {}", describe(value)),
        }));
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — nested/optional columns use the same minimum on server and device writes.
fn validate_column_minimum(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>, label: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => validate_column_minimum(inner, Some(value), label),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(items))) => {
            if let ColumnType::ListRange { min, .. } = kind { validate_list_minimum(label, items.len(), *min)?; }
            for item in items { validate_column_minimum(of, Some(item), label)?; }
            Ok(())
        }
        _ => Ok(()),
    }
}

fn validate_list_minimum(label: &str, length: usize, min: u32) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if length < min as usize {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} needs at least {min} {}; got {length}", if min == 1 { "item" } else { "items" })));
    }

            let audience = match &sync.audience {

//! Writes: the four words, their rules, the constraints (unique, cap,
//! immutable, append-only, after-insert), maintained aggregates, and the
//! partition events every row change produces.

use super::runner::Runner;
use super::validate::validate_row;
use crate::error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError};
use crate::partition;
use crate::rows::index_values;
use crate::rows::{Bounds, Dir};
use crate::schema::{Constraint, Effect, Table, materialize_optional_fields};
use crate::value::{Supplied, Value, canonical_json};

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum Origin {
    Source,
    Maintained,
    LiveAuthority,
}

#[derive(Clone)]
pub(super) struct FinalExistsWitness {
    table: String,
    row: Value,
    constraint: usize,
}

#[path = "write_references.rs"]
mod references;
#[path = "write_target.rs"]
pub(crate) mod target;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn insert(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, false).map(|_| ())
    }

    pub(super) fn insert_if_absent(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, true)
    }

    fn insert_mode(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        if self.device && if_absent && self.schema.tables[table_name].through.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("insert if absent on a read-through child queues without a row".into()));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            let mark = runner.meter.mark();
            let result = runner.insert_attempt(table_name, row, if_absent, None).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, Effect::Insert));
            // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — denied direct allocations hide deferred
            // read witnesses and counter work just as NOT_FOUND hides targets.
            if matches!(&result, Err(RuntimeError::Rule { effect: Effect::Insert, .. } | RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { .. }))
                && runner.schema.tables.get(table_name).is_some_and(|table| !table.sequences.is_empty()) {
                runner.meter.restore(mark);
            }
            result
        })
    }

    fn insert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool, upsert_index: Option<&str>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Insert)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, true)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        let supplied_sequence = super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row);
        // A directly supplied field is a payload error before any lookup;
        // only an upsert's copied address waits for insert authority.
        if upsert_index.is_none() && supplied_sequence.is_err() { return supplied_sequence.map(|_| false); }
        let supplied = row.clone();
        let row = self.with_defaults(table_name, row)?;
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].ephemeral.is_some() { self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes())?; }
        let row = row.ephemeral(&self.schema.tables[table_name], self.context.now.unwrap_or(0))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve, then stamp the parent capture before any rule sees the image.
        self.reserve_capture(&self.schema.tables[table_name])?;
        let row = self.capture_insert(table_name, row)?;
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        let independent = !table.sequences.is_empty() && !super::rules::deciding_fields(table_name, table, &table.rules.insert)
            .iter().any(|field| table.sequences.contains_key(field));
        // An independent denial must never consult a hidden read witness.
        let authorized = independent && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)?;
        if independent && !authorized {
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
        }
        if !table.sequences.is_empty() {
            // Admission reserves the worst case before either read verdict;
            // restoring a refusal alone cannot hide a boundary abort.
            // An independent insert rule was already prepaid and evaluated.
            // Only still-pending checks belong in this preflight allowance.
            let insert_checks = if authorized { 0 } else { 1 + u64::from(upsert_index.is_some() && supplied_sequence.is_ok()) };
            self.reserve_sequence_authorization(table, &row, insert_checks)?;
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — an unreadable allocation cannot expose counter,
        // uniqueness or cap state, even to a principal allowed to insert.
        if !self.sequence_insert_readable(table_name, &row)? {
            self.refusing_clause(table_name, "sequence read authority");
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "sequence read authority") });
        }
        if let Err(error) = supplied_sequence {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — absent upserts decide insert authority before field diagnostics.
            // A target key may supply an ordinal even when the patch does not.
            if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? {
                return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
            }
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { error });
        }
        if upsert_index.is_some() {
            self.authorize_fields(table_name, &row, &supplied).map_err(|_| self.no_write_target())?;
        } else { self.authorize_fields(table_name, &row, &supplied)?; }
        // The image copy an event slot costs is the writer's own supplied bytes (LLP 3000.015 §1).
        let image_bytes = row.heap_bytes();
        let (row, sequence_full) = self.sequence_fields(table_name, row)?;
        let row = self.receipt_insert_fields(table_name, row)?;
        if let Some(index) = upsert_index {
            if self.device { self.prove_upsert_insert(table_name, index, &row)?; }
            // Preserve upsert's target preflight before the ordinary insert checks;
            // only this first authority refusal is an undisclosed target miss.
            if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? { return Err(self.no_write_target()); }
            if sequence_full { return Err(super::sequences::exhausted()); }
            // Engine event slots are stamped after predecessor/sequence checks,
            // just as on an ordinary insert; preflight validates authored fields.
            if let Err(error) = super::validate::validate_before_slots(self.schema, table_name, &row) {
                return Err(if matches!(error, RuntimeError::Input(_)) && !self.context.is_system() { self.no_write_target() } else { error });
            }
        }
        if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? {
            return Err(self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule"));
        }
        // Retry keys independent of engine allocation still identify a no-op
        // at the ceiling. Never use the clamped authority candidate as a key.
        if sequence_full && if_absent {
            let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
            for (name, index) in table.indexes.iter().filter(|(_, index)| {

- **P1 — Re-entry can erase a saved reference’s lifetime binding.** [backfills.rs:228](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:228) selects the reference type from the current assembled source, then `capture_refs` deletes the existing private binding. Apply a backfill with a generic `id` argument originally sourced from a reference column; carry it while changing that column to `id`; remove and restore the table. Re-entry erases the original parent binding. After another removal, deleting/reusing the parent ID permits restoration to read the replacement parent and recompute outputs. Preserve the receipt’s original reference type/binding when consuming saved inputs. Add a regression covering both restoration cycles and parent deletion/reuse.

- **P2 — Rollback/forward loses omitted inputs’ original default interpretation.** [backfills.rs:333](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:333) reconstructs inactive receipts’ absent outputs but ignores their saved omitted-input values. For a physically omitted `n` interpreted as default `3`, apply `{doubled:n * 2}`, carry the declaration while changing the default to `100`, then select a source without the declaration/output but retaining default `100`. Forward selection computes `200` instead of preserving `6`, despite no authored input edit. Reconstruct saved interpretations for inactive receipts while lifetime omission provenance survives, including the lookup/index snapshot; add this rollback/forward regression.
